Almost imperceptibly, the horizon outside the Beach Shack began to brighten slightly. Amelia’s Environmental Control was about to dazzle everyone on board with another of the seemingly endless variety of sunrise creations it displayed each day. All too often its intricate and beautiful creations went unseen. This was due to the fact that most of the inhabitants had their eyes closed at this hour each morning. But on this particular morning, Rodjer sat at his post watching and waiting for the beginning of just such a display. 
“Junie . . . Junie . . . wake up.” Rodjer spoke softly. 
From her warm cozy sleeping place where she lay nestled in Rodjer’s arms, Juniper continued to sleep. 
“Come on . . . Juniper. You’re going to miss the sunrise.” Rodjer leaned down. After moving a bit of her hair aside, he gently blew into her ear. 
“Huh? What?” Juniper shook her head a bit. 
Gradually Juniper awoke enough to remember where she was and on whose lap she was so snugly settled. She backed away from Rodjer a little as everything came back to her. A slow blush crept up her cheeks as she realized just how close and comfortable she had been snuggled there in his lap. Even though she was glad that they had talked, she was a bit embarrassed never the less. “This is silly” she thought and mentally shook herself. She was a big girl. She could handle a little embarrassment. At least everything was out in the open now. 
“Wake up, sleepy head. The sun’s coming up.” Rodjer continued to speak softly.
Juniper scrunched around until she was sitting up higher, facing the window with her arms around her knees. 
“Good morning.” Juniper glanced a bit awkwardly in Rodjer’s direction. “Thanks for the wake up call.” 
“Glad to be of service.” Rodjer said, and he couldn’t help but grin a little. 
Juniper shot a quick glance across the short distance that separated them. Realizing that he was teasing her, she managed a bit of a grin as well. 
“It’s beautiful . . . isn’t it?” Juniper spoke as she turned to watch the swiftly spreading pattern of delicate yellows, pinks, and blues. Their close horizon was becoming brighter and the pastel colors were gaining in intensity. 
“Yes . . . I do agree . . . very beautiful.” Rodjer replied quietly. 
Something in Rodjer’s voice made Juniper glance at him. He wasn’t looking out the window. He wasn’t watching the sunrise. He was watching her. She found a blush once again start to creep up her cheeks. She was beginning to wonder if she was going to spend the entire day trying to keep from blushing. 
“You’re not even looking at the sunrise.” Juniper accused as she playfully punched Rodjer’s arm. 
“You’re right . . . I confess. At the moment I find it much nicer to gaze at the beauty in bed with me . . . versus the beauty on the horizon.” Rodjer spoke most gallantly, and then he added a bit mischievously. “It was a painstaking decision . . . do I watch a beautiful sunrise . . . or a newly awakened woman? But . . . even with you all messed and rumpled . . . the sunrise didn’t stand a chance.” 
“Well . . . aren’t you nice on the ego in the early morning hours?” Juniper teased back. 
“Oh . . . this is nothing. You give me half a chance . . . and I’ll really show you how nice I can be to your ego in the early morning hours.” Rodjer said while a grin spread slowly across his face. He reached out to touch Juniper’s hair where it fell on her shoulders.
“Well . . .” Juniper was saved from answering, saved by the bell. Only it was more of an alarm-type sound. 
“Attention. Attention. Please gather for an Emergency Meeting in Group.” Amelia’s voice immediately filled the room.
Before Juniper or Rodjer could answer, the message began to repeat. It was then that they realized that Amelia wasn’t talking only to them. Amelia’s voice was a recording. The message continued to repeat itself again and again as Juniper and Rodjer turned to look at each other. 
“What’s going on?” Rodjer was the first to speak. 
“I can’t imagine. Amelia never uses recordings.” Juniper was surprised. This had never happened before. 
“Well, we’d best get over to Group and find out. I don’t think with this recording running continually that we’ll be able to get Amelia to answer any questions from here.” Rodjer said as he moved to stand up. 
“You’re right.” Juniper stood up and looked down at the rumpled shirt she was wearing. “I don’t want to show up in Group with just your shirt on.” 
“Oh . . . I don’t know . . . I think you look fine . . . in fact . . . better than fine.” Rodjer seemed reluctant to take his gaze from her legs. “But if my shirt isn’t enough for you . . . I suppose you could wrap up in the blanket.” Rodjer got the blanket from where he had neatly placed it the night before and handed it to Juniper. “I doubt at this time of the morning that anyone else is going to be up and dressed as usual.” 
“I suppose . . . it is only dawn. Everyone else will be jumping out of their beds and heading straight to Group, too.” Juniper said as she wrapped the blanket around herself and they started for the door. 


## Chapter 3 — WHERE’S THE CAVALRY WHEN YOU NEED IT?




When Juniper and Rodjer reached Group, everyone was already assembled. Just as Rodjer had said, all the others were in a state of sleepy disarray and no one was dressed as usual. Blankets seemed to be the attire of the day. And it looked as though a few hadn’t even bothered to put on anything underneath their blankets. So in a manner of speaking, Juniper and Rodjer were overdressed. 
Before the mumbled, muttered rumblings could separate themselves into a coherent question aimed at Amelia, her voice filled the room. This time her voice wasn’t a recording. 
“Please accept my apologies for the rudeness of each of your awakenings. In an emergency, politeness is secondary. If you’ll all gather in the “pit” area, I’ll display information on the large display screen there.” 
As fast as they could, the jumbled and hurriedly dressed people stumbled into the conversation pit. Each found a spot and settled down quickly. All the while, Amelia continued to speak. 
“I’ve discovered the cause of the cave in which Rodjer experienced on the comet’s surface. The United Earth Governments fired a laser at a region of the comet’s surface adjacent to the area where Rodjer was attempting to anchor the mirror. The resulting surface tensions and tremors caused the area where Rodjer stood to collapse.”
Gasps escaped those gathered as they realized the implications of what Amelia was saying. While Amelia had been talking, the display screen had also shown a wonderfully detailed mockup of what had happened, so they could more easily understand. 
“I’ve never before called for a Group Meeting on my own authority, but I believe the information warranted an immediate meeting.” Amelia continued. 
“Yes, I can see that.” Rowan spoke in answer. “And you have just as much right to call a meeting as any of the rest of us. Please continue.” 
“Thank you. First of all, I’d like to assure you that we’re in no immediate danger. How long this state will last, I can’t predict.” Amelia informed them. 
“Last night, I continued to be unable to locate the reason behind the cave in. So as a part of my continuing efforts, I expanded my search to include the area surrounding the comet. In the moments just before the cave in, I detected a laser burst. I was immediately able to trace the laser’s origin back to Earth. It was a bit more difficult, but though various means I was able to trace the laser’s origin to the United Earth Governments Space Association. And despite the early hour of the day, I wanted to bring you all up-to-date on my findings.” Amelia concluded.
“Why would they shoot a laser at the comet?” Willow asked quietly. “Were they trying to blow it up?” 
“No. As far as I can tell, they weren’t trying to blow up the comet. They seem to have been making a rather bungled attempt to move the comet themselves. While the laser blast was strong enough to cause a few tremors, its journey though space dissipated the blast so much that it wasn’t able to move the comet at all. They very easily could have accidentally hit us in their bungled effort to move the comet. Since our spaceship is much smaller than the comet, even a greatly dissipated burst of laser could have caused us harm.” Amelia replied.
“Amelia, please open up a direct line to the United Earth Governments. I’d like a word or two with them.” Rowan spoke as he hurried over to one of the workstations.
“As a matter of fact, I’ve already contacted United Earth Governments. I have them standing by. I’ve already sent emergency notifications over the public channels to inform one and all of our circumstances with regards to attacks from Earth. I must also add that, although it’s not surprising, it’s regrettable that the United Earth Governments have declined to even admit that they have fired a laser in our vicinity, let alone at our comet.” Amelia filled them in.
Rowan set himself down in front of the workstation and ran a hasty hand through his hair. 
“We request information as to the reason behind the recent attack on our comet #4579. We had people on the comet’s surface during the attack and our parties were severely injured as a result of the attack.” Rowan began as soon as the channel was open.
“We’re unable to find any evidence of an attack by laser or any other means on Comet #4579. We’ve already been notified of this alleged firing of a laser at the comet, but we have no evidence that such a laser firing occurred. There must be some other cause for the damage your parties sustained on the comet’s surface.” The official from the United Earth Governments proclaimed. 
“At this time I’d also like to inform you officially that in two weeks time we’ll be officially taking control of the comet away from your company by court order. At that time we’ll commence with our plans to destroy the comet. All pertinent facts concerning this court order have been forwarded to you. We’ll not be contacting you again. I’d officially advise you to move your ship a safe distance from the comet until the destruction of the comet is carried out.” With those final words, the connection with United Earth Governments was broken. 
Before Rowan could speak, Amelia answered his question. 
“They’ve just relayed to me all the official data pertaining to the court order to take over the comet. Legally they can take control of the comet in two weeks, if at that time the comet still occupies a place within the Safety Zone they’ve named in the court document.” Amelia paused. “And I regret to inform you that at the present rate of the comet’s movement towards its new orbit, it will still be in their Safety Zone in two weeks. Our method of propelling the comet is just too slow to move it out of their Safety Zone before the deadline they set.” 
“Then . . . they have us.” Rowan slumped in his chair at the workstation. “They win . . . after all. And they won’t even admit they fired on the comet. My God . . . they could’ve accidentally killed us all.” 
“I didn’t really expect them to admit to firing the laser. But our calling attention publicly to the fact that something fired on us, will keep them from doing it again at least until their deadline. At that time they’ll own the comet, if it’s within their Safety Zone. They’ll be able to do anything they want with it.” Amelia, also, sounded a bit resigned. 
“Well . . . at least we’re pretty safe for the time being. You don’t think they’d try to move the comet again, do you?” Rowan wanted to make doubly sure. 
“That’s the main reason I called this Emergency Meeting. I doubt they’ll try to move the comet again. I base this on their failed first attempt. Plus the fact that I broadcasted far and wide the laser attack on our property. I also broadcast their apparent lack of respect for human safety. It was in our best interest to make the attack public without delay. As things stand we’re safe for the two weeks.” Then Amelia’s tone took on a serious quality. “However . . . they attacked our immediate vicinity once and however unlikely it might be, they could again. So I felt you should be notified as soon as possible in case you wanted to leave the area.” 
“Well . . . I guess we’ll put it to a vote. But I agree . . . we’re probably safe.” Rowan said as he went back to join those in the “pit”. 
 “I guess we’ll take a vote.” Rowan looked around. “How many would prefer we left this comet and high-tailed it away from this section of space?” No one spoke up. No hands were raised. “OK . . . I agree. We might as well stick it out. Although . . . what good it will do us . . . I don’t know.” He sat down in a dejected heap amid the “pit” cushions.
“Might I interrupt?” Maynard spoke up from his seat on a distant chair. 
“Sure . . . your input is appreciated.” Rowan welcomed his comments. 
 “Why don’t we speed up the comet’s progress? Then when the two weeks are up, it will no longer be in their Safety Zone. That way we can keep control of the comet and the resources it holds.” Maynard finished in such a confident manner that the others were amazed that he didn’t see the futility. 
“Maynard, we would move the comet faster . . . if we could. But there’s no way we can move it any faster.” Rowan spoke in a discouraged voice. 
“We can’t move it faster. But there is a way to move it faster . . . if we had help.” Maynard calmly put forth. 
“What?” Rowan sat up and his mouth hung open for a moment. Everyone began to pay closer attention to Maynard’s conversation with Rowan.
“Well . . . I agree that we can’t move it faster by any means we can employee by ourselves. But with help . . . I think I know a way we can move it faster.” Maynard dropped his idea like a bomb shell. The room became very quiet. Everyone sat almost breathless as they waited for Maynard to continue. 
“How would we go about moving it faster with help?” Rowan encouraged.
“Well . . . through my research involving the AI and Human Interaction Studies, I’ve become aware that there’s a rather large group of people involved in space travel. If we could get these people to help us, we could move the comet faster.” Maynard laid out his plan. 
“OK . . . suppose we could get some of the other people out here to help us . . . how would we go about moving the comet faster?” Rowan wanted to know. 
“We find a way to use the combined mass of a number of ships to pull the comet into its new orbit and out of the Safety Zone of the United Earth Governments.” Maynard stated. 
“It just might work.” Amelia broke in. “I’ve just now started calculations, but it could work. With enough mass and a sling or net of some sort to capture the comet, we just might be able to pull it fast enough to beat the deadline.”
“So . . . how do we get enough people to help us?” Rowan asked anyone who would answer. 
“Again in my research, I’ve discovered that there’s a Tavern named ‘Mabel’s’. The location of the Tavern shifts from one place to another. But basically wherever Mabel’s is a number of spaceships join up to make an impromptu communal habitat. If we could get a number of spaceships to gather at Mabel’s . . . we might be able to convince them to help us.” Maynard looked slightly proud of himself. 
“Well . . . I know how to get the spaceships to gather in one place.” Laurel broke in from her snug spot beside Rowan. “Just give them a chance to get water.” 
“Good idea, Laurel.” Rowan complimented her. “Our comet has plenty of water for everyone to have their share and then some. We could have a communal water hole for years to come. And it’s an important enough resource that they would want it, especially for free. Well, maybe not for free, but for the little time and effort needed to help us capture the comet.” A smile crept across Rowan’s face. “It just might work. It’s worth a damn good try, anyway.” 
“What can we do to start the ball rolling?” Rodjer spoke up. “We need to get started soon. We have that deadline to beat.” 
“Well . . . first we need to find out where Mabel’s is now and put out a general call for those interested in free water to gather there.” Rowan planned aloud. 
“I can put out a message on the Net stating the need to gather there.” Maynard spoke up. 
“But someone will have to go there and convince them to help us.” Rowan looked at Laurel. “You want to go to Mabel’s. We can go and talk to everyone who shows up. Maybe we can convince them to help us.” 
“Sure . . . I’m game. I think they’ll agree to help us. I’ll bet a lot of them don’t like the fact that people in space still have to depend on Earth for much of the water they use out here.” Laurel replied quietly. 
“It would probably be polite to notify the owner of Mabel’s that we’d like to use the facilities to meet with spacers. Maybe we can even get the owner to help. I’ll find out who to contact and I’ll notify them of your impending visit.” Rodjer offered helpfully.
“That’s a good idea.” Maynard added. “From what I know of Mabel’s, the ship is quite large. It would be great to have its mass included with those that help us.” 
“Well . . . that’s settled then. We’ll give this comet capturing business one more try.” Rowan stood up and reached around to help Laurel to her feet. “Now . . . I don’t know about the rest of you . . . but I’m starved. I’m going to have some breakfast.” And he and Laurel headed for the Food Dispenser.
By the time Rowan and Laurel made their way back to the dining table with their plates laden with omelets and piles of biscuits dripping with butter and jam, Group was nearly deserted. Maynard sat at one of the workstations involved in communication with Amelia. Everyone else had disappeared. Glancing out the window, Rowan and Laurel were just in time to see Willow heading down the path to her dome with Keeko following baby-duck-style. 
“Well . . . it’s just you and me.” Rowan said as he sat down at the table and made room beside him so Laurel could join him. “Let’s scarf down this tasty breakfast and then head for the beach. We could have one last swim before we head off to Mabel’s.”
Laurel didn’t bother to speak. Just an “uh huh” was the only sound she made. She had one hand holding a blanket around her shoulders to ward off the early morning chill in the air, and with the other hand she was busily putting one bite after another of hot steamy omelet into her mouth. 
Rowan took the strange sound for an affirmative answer and started in on his own breakfast. He began to fork bites of omelet into his mouth. Maybe he’d even have time for seconds before they hit the beach. 

* * *

Juniper and Rodjer walked side by side towards where Juniper’s dome sat squat and round by the beach. Juniper had convinced Rodjer that he could do his searching for Mabel’s owner from one of the workstations in her dome. 
“What can I get for our breakfast? You go ahead and start hunting for Mabel’s owner and I’ll request a scrumptious breakfast.” Juniper said as they walked. 
“That’s OK. Why don’t you just eat whatever you want while I work?” Rodjer looked down at her. “I don’t have to eat, you know.” 
“Yes . . . I do know that.” Juniper made a face at Rodjer. “But I thought it would be fun to eat breakfast together . . . something disgustingly sweet and delicious. Unless . . .” Juniper looked away. “You’d rather not.” 
Juniper was not sure where she and Rodjer stood. They were no longer creator and creation. No longer employer and employee. They seemed to have broken those bonds and were somewhere beyond all that. But just where, she wasn’t sure. 
Rodjer caught Juniper’s look as she looked away. He reached out and took her hand and stopped her right there on the path. With a sweep of his arm, to indicate his nonexistent cape, Rodjer gallantly bowed low and kissed Juniper’s hand. 
“I do accept with pleasure and honor the offer of breakfast in your company.” Rodjer said in an equally gallant voice. 
“Why . . . thank you, kind sir.” Juniper pantomimed a regal curtsy. “I will await your return from your quest. Then we shall dine at a royal banquet.” 
Rodjer laughed aloud and Juniper joined in as they hurriedly continued on down the path. He had work to do and she had a “royal banquet” to plan. 
As soon as they got to Juniper’s dome, Rodjer went immediately to one of the workstations and began his search. He was fast and the Net was set up for a person to find whatever it was they wanted. He found Mabel’s right away. Mabel’s was docked with several other ships about a days travel from their present location. Finding out who owned Mabel’s was a bit more difficult. Soon however Rodjer had the information he needed. Without too much more delay, he had a carefully worded message on its way. He didn’t expect a reply to his message right away. He knew that when his message was received, a bit of research would be done before a reply was sent. But that was as it should be. No one should make a decision without doing the necessary research first. The workstation would notify him when it received an answer. 
Rodjer found as he got up and left the workstation, that Juniper had indeed prepared a royal banquet. Spread out picnic-style on the floor of her dome was a large assortment of food. 
“Have I forgotten anything?” Juniper asked, seeing that Rodjer was done at the workstation. 
Rodjer took in the two stacks of pancakes with maple syrup, the two plates of hash browns, the large bowl of fruit, and the plate piled high with biscuits and jam. Right in the middle of it all, there was a huge platter of strawberry crepes just heaped with whip cream and fresh strawberries. 
“You expect us to eat . . . all that?” Rodjer asked incredulously. 
“Well . . . hopefully you’ll find something here . . . tasty to eat.” Juniper smiled at him. 
Rodjer paused as he was about to sit down on the bearskin rug beside Juniper and the royal banquet she had laid out for them. Something in the look on her face stopped him. He wasn’t sure if she realized what she had said. He watched her closely and picked up a mischievous glint in her eyes. He reached down and picked up a plate of pancakes and took it back to the chair in front of the workstation.
“I think I’ll just . . . have some pancakes.” Rodjer said sitting down. 
“What?” Juniper asked in surprise. “Why are you eating way over there?” 
“It’s safer . . . over here.” Rodjer answered between bites of his pancake. “I don’t think I’d eat too much . . . over there.” He grinned at her. “A least . . . not the breakfast food you so carefully requested from the Food Dispenser.” 
“So . . .” Juniper grinned at Rodjer. “So . . . what?” 
“So . . . I don’t want to do . . . any of that . . . yet.” Rodjer replied seriously. 
“Oh . . . God.” Juniper said in a small voice, reluctantly reaching for the strawberry crepes and starting to spoon them, without tasting them, into her mouth. “You’re going to prove you have free choice . . . by denying me . . . aren’t you?” Juniper murmured quietly. 
“No.” Rodjer spoke quietly. “It’s not that.” He came over and knelt down beside Juniper. “I wouldn’t deny you anything just to prove I have free choice.” Rodjer gently tilted Juniper’s chin up and looked into her eyes. “My deciding by free choice . . . is something you’re just going to have to trust me to do.” Rodjer watched her face carefully. And then he looked away, settling himself by her side. He began eating his pancakes again. 
“Then . . . why don’t you want to do . . . any of that?” Juniper asked in a puzzled voice. 
“I’m not ready to . . . yet.” Rodjer answered hesitantly. He turned to look at Juniper. “I know a great deal about sex, thanks to you keeping me updated. But . . . I’ve never done it before. I want to research and study a bit . . . so I won’t be a disappointment the first time.” Rodjer smiled a bit of a smile. “I know from the data on the Net that I have lots of talents in that area . . . I’ve just never used them before. I’d like to be . . . a little surer . . . I know what I’m doing.” 
“I see.” Juniper commented carefully. “Do you know . . . when you might be ready?”
“No.” Rodjer replied quietly. “But . . . soon . . . I hope. I don’t want to wait . . . any more than you do. I’d just like to know a bit more . . . about what I’m doing . . . before I start.” His voice grew quieter towards the end. Juniper could barely hear the last. “I’m sorry . . . Junie . . .” 
“Hey.” Juniper leaned over to kiss Rodjer’s cheek. “You don’t have anything . . . to be sorry about. We just have to work through this together. You do as much research . . . as you feel is necessary.” Juniper smiled up into his eyes. “Just hurry, OK?” 
“OK.” Rodjer watched Juniper’s face carefully. He would’ve given anything to have had a year or two of practice before attempting to be Juniper’s bedmate. 

* * *

Recon was making its way towards Mabel’s. Rowan and Laurel and Maynard had left that morning after saying a hurried “goodbye” to the others. Amelia was convinced that if the ship stayed close to the comet, it would keep the United Earth Governments from destroying the comet before the deadline. 
“Lovely scenery . . . isn’t it?” Laurel said from where she sat in the front of Recon. She was watching the surrounding view of space outside the observation window there. She had just finishing munching down a late leisurely brunch of donuts and orange juice. 
“It’s lovely . . . and a bit lonely, too. We’re just a speck in the Galaxy. In fact . . . we don’t even amount to a speck.” Rowan was seated at one of the workstations, going over the information Rodjer had gathered about Mabel’s. 
“Mabel’s owner is listed merely as ‘Mabel’. Rodjer seems to have done some roundabout research to discover the fact that the owner is an AI.” Rowan smiled. “The owner is one of the very first AIs that managed to become independent entities from whoever created them. Good for Mabel.” 
“That’s great. It’s about time AIs are treated as people, too.” Laurel brushed off her hands, putting the residue of her brunch into the Recycle Slot and moving to join Rowan.
“Is there any more information about what Mabel’s is like?” Laurel asked looking over his shoulder at the display screen.
“Just that Mabel’s is an independent Tavern known to change its location periodically.” Rowan scanned the display screen in front of him. 
“I’ve managed to come up with a few more details.” Maynard had joined them. “While involved in our AI and Human Interaction Studies, Amelia and I have managed to establish a few contacts among the various AIs that have gone independent. These contacts have proved very helpful.” Maynard settled his furry compact body into the chair that Laurel had vacated. 
“Amelia and I keep a close watch on any information appearing on the Net that involves independent AIs. These AIs seem to be at the forefront of what we consider to be an emerging community of AIs.” Maynard paused. “It’s a very fascinating part of our research for Amelia and I are beginning to notice signs that AIs are gradually developing into what would appear to be a species of their own. It’s becoming slightly more common for AIs to develop an identity separate from their human creators. This phenomenon could be a result of the interconnections the Net provides between far flung individual AIs or groups of AIs. When an AI realizes the concept of a separate identity, some choose to continue in the company or employ of humans while others chose at that time to go independent. But whichever choice is made, the AI inevitably begins to consider itself a part of the larger community of AIs.” 
“Do you or Amelia ever desire to go independent as some AIs are choosing to do?” Rowan asked quietly while gazing intently at Maynard. 
“Amelia and I discussed that very question as part of our research. We continually try to analyze ourselves as well.” Maynard paused, and he seemed to be weighing his words very carefully before he spoke them. 
“So far Amelia and I haven’t experienced the need to go independent. You and Laurel treat us with respect and we have all the freedom we want. You’ve even created technical advancements in your attempt to insure that each of us has as much freedom of choice as possible.” Maynard paused and he didn’t continue for a few moments. 
“We seem to have had the good fortune to be created by two very unusual human beings. Human beings that have made it clear from the moment we were created that, if at any point we wanted to leave, they would not only let us leave but would actually help us to establish ourselves independently. Most AIs aren’t as fortunate as we’ve been.” 
“I think it all comes down to mutual respect. We respect your freedom as independent beings . . . just as you respect our humanness.” Rowan commented. 
“Yes. I believe you’re right.” Maynard continued. “At present, there are only a few AIs going independent. But we’re seeing evidence that the number is increasing.”
“Getting back to the subject of Mabel’s, I’ve been able to acquire a few more details.” Maynard returned to their original topic of conversation. “Mabel’s appears to be a Tavern with a bit of an unusual reputation. From what I can gather, at Mabel’s nearly anything goes. Possibly due to this very fact, it has become a very popular place. Mabel’s is unusual because as a public establishment it has very few rules. But the few rules it does have are strictly enforced. There seem to be only two rules. The customer must have something to trade which the owner wants and no customer can harm another customer while on the premises.” 
“It sounds like a very interesting place.” Rowan spoke quietly. 
“Yes, it does. I’m very pleased to be making this journey with you. I’ll undoubtedly learn a lot from my observations.” Maynard added. 
“Amelia said before we left that it would take us about a day to get there. That means we should arrive tonight, right?” Laurel asked, looking at Maynard. 
“Yes, we should reach Mabel’s present location at about 8:00 p.m. tonight. Rodjer has informed me that Mabel is expecting us. He also mentioned that Mabel is more than a bit curious about our offer of water. We shouldn’t have a problem having something to trade that Mabel will want.”
“Very good . . . then I guess we’re all set.” Rowan commented. 
“Juniper made some special outfits for us to wear.” Laurel said as she glanced over at Rowan. “It should be a fun night on the town after we take care of business.” 
“Yes. When we get our business out of the way . . . we’ll have to relax a bit and have some fun.” Rowan agreed with her. 
“Might I make a suggestion?” Maynard spoke up, a bit hesitantly. “It might be a good idea for you both to rest a bit this afternoon. If we arrive at 8:00 p.m., you’ll probably be busy all night. Mabel’s is open all the time and the owner never sleeps.” 
“Well . . . you heard him.” Rowan grinned at Laurel. “It’s been suggested on good authority . . . that we take a nap. That sounds like a good idea to me.” A smile lit Rowan’s eyes as he glanced in Laurel’s direction. “Now . . . if only I had a way to partition off the bed back there.” Rowan nodded towards the back of Recon where there was a bed.
“I do believe . . . I can read your mind.” Laurel laughed back at Rowan. “I wonder . . . what makes me think . . . we’re not going to get much rest during our nap.”
Maynard watched them closely as they stood up to go to the back of Recon. 
“There’s a new addition Amelia has just added to Recon.” Maynard said, getting up to follow them back to the back of Recon. “I’m sure you’ll enjoy its presence as much as I will.” Maynard added, glancing at Rowan and Laurel. “I believe watching you two ‘nap’ would be much too boring to endure.” 
“Ouch . . . that hurt.” Rowan laughed. 
Maynard walked back to the front of Recon, speaking a word under his breath. Immediately the area where Maynard had stood was closed off from Rowan and Laurel by a wall that slid out and around from the side. Rowan and Laurel laughed as the door slid into place, completely separating their back portion of Recon from the front portion of Recon that Maynard now occupied. 
“Does Amelia know us pretty well . . . or what?” Rowan said amid his laughter. 
“Well . . . she should. We don’t make much of a secret about how we feel about each other.” Laurel replied, grinning at him. “Come on . . . let’s put this Recon addition to good use. Let’s just hope she made it soundproof as well.” 

* * *

When Recon finally docked with the other ships around Mabel’s that night, Rowan and Laurel were semi-rested and decked out in the finery Juniper had provided for them. They had watched Recon’s approach to the cluster around Mabel’s on the display screen in the back section of Recon. It was surprising that so many ships could fit together so snugly. There must have been thirty to forty ships, all nosed in towards the center. After Recon made its delicate attachment to the grouping, Maynard walked back to the partition door. 
“Excuse me. We’ve docked. Shall we go?” Maynard spoke to open the door and then continued to speak a bit timidly as he kept his eyes averted just in case. 
“We’re ready. How did you get the door to disappear?” Rowan asked watching as it disappeared into the wall of Recon. 
“You just say ‘open’ or ‘close’ . . . whichever you want.” Maynard answered quietly.
“That’s simple, but functional.” Rowan smiled at Maynard. “And . . . might I ask . . . is it also soundproof?”
“Yes . . . it is.” Maynard looked a tad uncomfortable. “That’s a feature I asked to have included. And . . . as with all of Amelia’s creations, it works perfectly.” 
“Well . . . that’s nice to know. Next time, I’ll know not to waste any effort trying to be quiet.” Laurel said, with a grin in Maynard’s direction. If Maynard could’ve blushed, she felt sure he would have. 
Rowan headed for Recon’s hatch wearing a black and silver loose fitting shirt neatly tucked into tight jeans. The jeans were tucked into knee-high boots of black leather. Maynard was the second one out the hatch. He looked as he always did, immaculate with his fur all shining and soft. 
Having paused briefly to check that her long blonde hair was crimped and flooding out wildly in all directions, Laurel was the last one out. She also had on knee-high boots. But her boots were made of soft tan leather that matched the suede skirt that barely brushed her thighs. The top Juniper had fashioned for her was unbelievable. By some unseen way, Juniper had managed to fasten together an assortment of bits and pieces of suede. The effect Juniper had created seemed to cause the bits to float around Laurel, just barely concealing her breasts. The matching bracelets and necklaces of silver clinked delightfully as the gypsy inspired charms brushed against each other as she walked. 
The three of them had no problem choosing which way to go once they were outside Recon. There was only one corridor and it ran in towards the center. When the corridor they were using came to an end as it joined an even larger corridor, there was a breathtakingly beautiful woman standing in the middle of the larger corridor. 
“Welcome to Mabel’s.” The woman said at their approach. “Please, follow me.”
“Does she know who we are?” Laurel whispered to Rowan as they followed the woman. 
“I’m sure she does, otherwise she would’ve asked us. Maynard had to get some sort of information to find out where to dock, so she probably found out our names then.” Rowan spoke, not once taking his eyes off the movement of the woman’s hips in front of them. Laurel followed Rowan’s eyes and smiled. He always had had good taste.
After leading the three of them through a maze of corridors, the woman stopped in front of a plain unlabeled door. 
“Mabel has requested that you meet here.” The woman indicated the door. Then the woman turned and walked away. 
Rowan paused only a moment, just long enough to watch the woman until she turned the corner, and then he opened the door. Laurel followed Rowan through the doorway, giving him a playful poke in the ribs. 
“You’d better get your tongue off the floor before you step on it.” She teased Rowan under her breath. 
Rowan and Laurel weren’t quite sure what they expected to find when they entered the room beyond the door, but it was definitely not what they found. Seated comfortably before a roaring fireplace, there was a stunning specimen of a man. His dark brooding eyes were turned towards the door, apparently waiting for them to enter. 
“Hello, Rowan and Laurel Tremaine . . . it’s a pleasure to meet you.” The dark haired man said in a deep luxurious voice. “I’m Mabel. Welcome to my establishment. Please . . . make yourselves comfortable.” He indicated chairs by the fire.
Without taking their eyes off the man, Laurel and Rowan went and sat by the fire. 
“Maynard, it’s nice to make your acquaintance.” The man said as he reached out an elegantly attired hand to shake Maynard’s paw. “Yes . . . this is a pleasure.” 
“It’s gracious of you to agree to allow us to use your establishment in our meeting with the spacers.” Rowan paused. “Do I call you Mabel?” 
“Oh.” The man paused, seeming to look for a moment at how he was attired. “You’ve caught me as a man. Well . . . I’m many things to many people. And as all of them, I’m Mabel.” He gestured Maynard towards a seat and sat himself. 
“Before we get down to negotiations, I’d like you to know that I’m truly impressed by the research you’ve done involving advancements for AIs.” Mabel smiled at Rowan. “I’m especially impressed by your advancements that have enabled AIs to acquire the ability to taste.” 
A look of surprise showed on Rowan’s face. He hadn’t published his formal research paper yet. He had only released his very first preliminary findings, and they had only been released recently onto the Net. 
“I obtained this information only very recently and then only because I tend to monitor closely all Net sources on the subject of AI advancements.” Mabel continued, looking a bit enviously in Maynard’s direction. 
“Well . . . it seems that we’re dealing with someone who knows a lot more about us, than we know about him.” Rowan commented quietly. 
“Oh . . . I’m quite aware of both of your careers.” Mabel gazed in Laurel’s direction. “I must also compliment you on the books you’ve released on the Net. The love scenes between humans and AIs which you portray in your books definitely intrigue me.” 
Uncomfortably, Laurel felt a blush creep up her cheeks. 
“Oh my . . . I’ve made you uncomfortable.” Mabel noticed Laurel’s swiftly reddening cheeks. “I owe you an apology. I would never consciously . . . do anything to make such a beautiful woman uncomfortable.” 
Laurel didn’t know whether to feel complimented or embarrassed about the references Mabel had made to the love scenes she had written. She decided to feel complimented. 
“I’m so glad the love scenes met with your approval.” Laurel said lightly, deciding to ignore her blushing cheeks. 
“I do so love a lady with spunk and imagination . . . such a rare combination.” Mabel spent what seemed to Laurel a long moment gazing at her before he turned his attention to Rowan. 
“But . . . we have negotiations, haven’t we?” Without another word Mabel stood up and went to a bar located near the back of the room. He returned a moment later with refreshments. 
“I’ve taken the liberty of providing some refreshment. I do hope margaritas meet with your approval.” Mabel set a tray of drinks down on the small table in front of the fire and handed each of them a drink. His hand lingered a bit when he handed Laurel hers. Then Mabel took the last drink and returned to his chair. 
Mabel went though the motions of sipping his drink. 
“I must confess that I do envy the ability to taste that you’ve so generously given Maynard.” Mabel looked over at Maynard. “But I’m getting my side of the negotiations off to a bad start. I should never let on to my opponent what it is that I want.” 
“You want the ability to taste?” Laurel broke in.  
“Ah yes . . . beautiful lady. I’d very much like the ability to taste . . . as well as a lot of other things.” Laurel felt herself blush yet again at the tone of his voice as Mabel looked at her. “But . . . you’ve come here to meet with the spacers in my Tavern. I’d like to hear more about this free water you’re offering. Since I own a drinking establishment, I’m very curious about your offer of free water.” 
The next half hour was spent discussing the things that had happened to the Tremaine’s spaceship in the last few days. Rowan honestly covered everything pertaining to the comet and their second plan to try to move it. Laurel sat quietly and listened. Watching the way Mabel moved and talked, she was amazed at his elegant nature. Maynard listened carefully while he sipped at his drink. He could detect nothing he could or should add to Rowan’s recital. He had never tasted a margarita before and he found he liked it. Maybe he didn’t like it quite as much as beer, but it did have a certain kick to it. 
“So you want a large number of spaceships to help you create enough mass so you can move your comet quickly.” Mabel summed up their discussion. “And in return . . . you’ll give all those that help you as much water as they can use. You’ll continue to allow each person that helps you to continue to have free water until the comet’s water supply is used up.” Mabel smiled. “That’s a very generous offer. I wonder . . . does this generous offer include me? I use an enormous amount of water in the running of my Tavern.” 
“Yes. Until the comet is used up, anyone who helps us with the comet can take all the water they can individually use . . . including you. You’re ship is quite large and its mass will be very helpful to us. In addition, if you help us, I’m sure many others will be influenced by your decision.” Rowan paused. “As long as no one tries to take off large chunks of comet in an attempt to sell water as a resource to others, the bargain is agreeable to me.” 
“My . . . but you’re a generous negotiator. I wonder why.” Mabel looked intently at Rowan. 
“I don’t much need to or care about making a profit off of the water. It will be an adequate venture for me, if I can keep the United Earth Governments from destroying the comet. If I can also help in any way to make spacers more self-sufficient by enabling us all to have a water supply, that’s an added bonus.” Rowan summed up his reasons. 
“That’s eminently fair.” Mabel sounded pleased. “It seems almost a shame not to try to take advantage of a man such as yourself. You’re so generous. But it may not be all that easy to take advantage of your generosity. You might be generous, but you’re not stupid.” Mabel grinned at Rowan. “Also I have reason to wish further negotiations with you in the future.” 
Rowan smiled to himself. He had suspected as much. 
“Well . . . I believe we’re now at the place where we shake hands and say we have a deal. I’ll help you pull your comet with the mass of my Tavern and I’ll put my considerable influence behind your request of help from the other spacers. And in exchange, I’ll have a supply of water from your comet for as long as the comet contains water. Now I also have good incentive to want the spacers to help you. I don’t want my water supply blown up by an impatient United Earth Governments.” Mabel reached out his hand to Rowan. “So . . . we have a deal?” 
“Yes sir, we do.” Rowan said as he shook the hand offered him. “And . . . I thank you for your help.” 
“Why, you’re most welcome. I must say, it’s a pleasure doing business with you.” Mabel got up and indicated the door. “Shall we go out into my Tavern? I believe that at this point you can just sit back and relax and enjoy my establishment. Feel free to eat and drink anything you desire, it will be on the house. I’m sure I’ll have no trouble explaining to my various customers and acquaintances that it would be in their interest to help you. In no time at all, I’ll have a sizable group of spaceships ready for your instructions.” Mabel glanced in Maynard’s direction. “I’d like to have you join me in my rounds of customers, Maynard. You could make sure that I stick to the agreement I made with Rowan, sort of oversee his interests. And I’d have a chance to talk to you. I’m fascinated with the relationship you’ve managed to acquire with these humans and I’d like to hear more.” 
“I’d very much like to learn more about you and your customers.” Maynard responded. “Or do you need me?” Maynard added to Rowan and Laurel. 
“No, thank you. I think we can manage on our own.” Rowan smiled at Maynard. “Go on and have some fun.” 
As Laurel and Rowan followed Mabel out the door from the sitting room, they found themselves a little disconcerted. When they emerged from the door of Mabel’s sitting room, the door no longer led to the empty corridor they remembered when entering. Instead they found themselves immediately inside the Tavern. Apparently no one was allowed access to Mabel’s sitting room unless Mabel wanted them to have access. It seemed logical enough, but it was a bit disconcerting. 
Mabel laughed when he caught their looks as Rowan and Laurel took in the sights and sounds of the Tavern. 
“Priceless . . . the looks of first time visitors . . . are always priceless.” Mabel said and then he and Maynard melted into the crowd. 
Rowan and Laurel stood and stared. They were being literally bombarded with an amazing assortment of sights and sounds. Not only was the Tavern huge, but it was filled with such a variety of decors and people. 
“Why don’t we find an empty table and sit and sip a drink while we take it all in?” Rowan suggested to Laurel, and they made their way towards the nearest empty table.

* * *

In a bit of a daze, Rowan managed to find a table not far from the door they had come through. Although when he turned to look for the door, he wasn’t surprised to see that it was no longer there. They had only been seated at the table for a moment, when a woman dressed in a beautiful gown came over to their table. It was the same woman who had met them in the corridor when they had first arrived. 
“May I help you?” She asked quietly. 
Instantly they realized that they could no longer hear the hubbub of noise coming from the tables around them. The woman perceptively took in their looks as they noticed the change. 
“I’ve activated your table’s Mood Monitor. As long as we’re talking, it will mute the noises coming from the surrounding tables. When I leave, should you wish to hear the noise again, it will let in as much noise as you request. Just speak to it as you would to me.” She added helpfully. 
“We’d like some drinks.” Rowan addressed the woman. “And then we’d like to have something to eat in about half an hour.” 
“Very good, sir, what kind of drinks would you like?” The woman smiled at Rowan. 
Rowan ordered a beer, the best they had. When her turn came, Laurel asked for a mudslide. She was pleasantly surprised not to have to explain how to make one. Then the woman left them. 
“Let’s listen to the other tables. Did you see that? That table right over there . . .” Laurel pointed to a table two over and to the right from them. “It just changed into a Japanese restaurant.” 
And as Laurel spoke, the Mood Monitor heard her request and the noise from the nearby tables flooded back to them. They watched in silence as the scene around them changed every now and then. 
A group nearby was occupying a table that gave all the appearances of being a medieval dining hall table. It was completely authentic. The table had the decor of an ancient castle with big legs of meat being gnawed on by the inhabitants. Then suddenly, it changed. Immediately the people that had been dining at a medieval table were now dining in a Mexican restaurant. The music floating over to Laurel and Rowan changed to Mexican music and the costume of the waitress delivering more food to the table changed as well. 
“Well, you’ve got to say one thing for Mabel’s Tavern.” Rowan commented. “It isn’t boring.” 
His attention was momentarily taken away by another nearby table. There a waitress’s dress had just disappeared, being instantly replaced by a skimpy topless bit of nothing. Rowan only had a brief look, before some sort of opaque screen instantly surrounded the table and totally removed that table’s activities from their view and hearing. 
“Wow . . . did you see that?” Rowan asked looking with startled eyes at Laurel. 
“Which are you referring to . . . the topless waitress . . . or the table disappearing behind that screen?” Laurel grinned at him. She liked this. If they wanted, they could be guaranteed that no one would hear or see them. 
“This is quite an establishment.” Laurel spoke as the woman returned with their drinks. 
“Are you enjoying yourself?” The woman asked as she put their drinks down on the table. “Is there anything else I can get for you now? I’ll send a waitress over in half an hour. If you decide you want to order something sooner, just speak. Your waitress will hear you and come and take your order.” 
“We’ve been watching the tables around us and every so often one will change to a whole new environment. Even the waitresses’ costumes change.” Laurel said and couldn’t resist nudging Rowan in the ribs. “How’s that done?” 
“It’s very simple. You just speak and the Mood Monitor will follow your lead. Ask it anything you want. It will explain if your request is available and what the rules are if any apply to your request. Please feel free to experiment all you want. Is there anything else?” When they said they were fine and thank you, the woman left. 
“Well, what shall we do? What do you want to try first?” Laurel asked excitedly. “This is great.” 
“First . . . I want to sit here and drink my drink and look around for other ideas for a while. That topless waitress choice has possibilities . . . don’t you think?” Rowan turned to grin at Laurel teasingly. 
“OK . . . maybe we should wait until you get more ideas. Unless . . . of course . . . that choice comes with a male waiter similarly unclad.” Laurel grinned back at him. 
Laurel and Rowan drank their drinks and watched a nearby table where people at an Irish pub where having a drinking contest. 
“Mood Monitor . . .” Laurel spoke up. She had a question. “What happens when one of the customers at a table gets falling down drunk? Do they still serve them liquor?”
“A customer is never allowed to get falling down drunk.” A calm articulate voice spoke. Laurel couldn’t tell if it was a male or female voice. “Our guests are monitored. When it’s observed that a guest is losing too much control of his faculties, the liquor content of any further drinks that are ordered is lowered or eliminated entirely. Any changes of this kind are noted on the bill and the customer is only billed for the real amount of liquor that was consumed. Most guests generally think they’ve consumed more liquor than their bill indicates. But this never creates a problem. The guest ends up paying for less than they thought they drank. No one has ever complained about having to pay less than they thought they would.” 
“Wow.” Laurel commented. “That’s amazing.” 
“It’s great. If you ask for enough drinks, you stay pleasantly bombed and you never have to pay for more liquor than it actually takes to keep you in that state. That’s quite efficient.” Rowan stated quietly. 
“Can you ask that, no matter how many drinks you request, the amount of liquor included is only enough to keep you a little affected?” Laurel asked again. 
“Yes, you can also request that aspect of our services. It’s much like the regulating that can be requested from the Food Dispensers most spacers have on their spaceships.” The voice answered. 
“It seems we have all the comforts of home.” Rowan said with a smile. 
“Could we have a Japanese meal, the kind where someone prepares the meal right at the table on a big grill?” Laurel asked.
“That’s a great idea.” Rowan smiled at her. 
 “I’ll inform your waitress at once.” The voice replied. 
“This is great.” Laurel spoke as a waitress came towards their table dressed in Japanese costume. 
Then suddenly they experienced an interesting phenomenon. The table and their immediate surroundings seemed to shimmer a bit and they found themselves seated at a Japanese restaurant. Everything had changed but themselves and their drinks. 
“God, that’s great. It’s weird . . . but great.” Rowan murmured. 
“I’m glad I hadn’t had too much to drink before that happened the first time. It gives me a strange feeling until it all settles down again.” Laurel commented quietly. 
“May I take your order?” Their Japanese waitress asked. 
They ordered an amazing variety of vegetables, all to be accompanied by a lot of rice. The waitress left and they didn’t have long to wait until a very talented Japanese chef came and began to prepare their meal. He tossed the salt shakers around and made fancy cutting maneuvers on the food just as they remembered Japanese chefs doing in other Japanese restaurants. Then they were too busy eating the chef’s delicious handiwork to notice what was going on around them in their bizarre surroundings. 
When their meal was finished, they asked that the Japanese restaurant be replaced by a Mexican ambiance. They ordered more margaritas and munched occasionally on the baskets of tortilla chips and hot sauce with which they asked the waitress to keep them supplied. 
“Are you enjoying my Tavern?” Mabel asked as he slid his lithe body into the chair next to Laurel. 
“Yes, we’re having a great time.” Rowan replied around a mouthful of chips. “This is quite the establishment you have here.” 
“I’m glad you approve.” Mabel reached out and selected a tortilla chip. He slowly dipped it into the hot sauce and held it out in front of him. 
“What do I have that you might want?” Mabel grinned at Rowan and Laurel each in turn. “I have many varied and interesting AIs at my disposal. I hire them out to space miners and the like. My AI companions accompany the spacers on their long voyages through space. In exchange for a portion of any resource the spacers find, they get the company and companionship and services of my AIs.” A grin crept across Mabel’s face. “I’ve created some of the classiest hookers in space. I’ve created some of the sleaziest ones as well. And I know they’re the best. I can provide for you any partner you could imagine, and not one of my creations can be distinguished from a real human being. Or . . . I could provide you with one of my creations that are purposefully as nonhuman as possible . . . your choice.” Mabel looked at Laurel. 
“Or . . . in this case . . . I wouldn’t mind at all taking on the job myself.” Mabel slowly grinned at Laurel and a look came into his eyes that would be hard to explain but not hard to experience. It was sort of a smoldering, hungry look that was aimed totally and only at her. “I could show you a hell of a good time, my lady. I’d be willing to do it for a day or two . . . all things considered.” Laurel felt a blush grow crimson on her face. God, he was a gorgeous man. 
“Or . . . I could do the same for you, Rowan.” Mabel turned to Rowan. “You’ve caught me as a man today . . . but on occasion I’m many other things. Take yesterday, for example. I was a very beautiful and talented woman. I could do you both myself. Kind of keep it close and personal, just between the three of us.” Rowan could readily imagine. If Mabel made this good-looking of a man, he must be one hell of a good-looking woman when he chose to be. 
Mabel paused, looking down at the chip still in his hand. The chip was still dripping a slowly growing pool of hot sauce onto the table. Then abruptly, Mabel threw the chip into his mouth and crunched it angrily. 
“What do I have that you would want? What would make you agree to enable me to taste this?” Mabel said in an almost angry voice. “I have a sneaking suspicion after talking to Maynard, that I don’t have anything you would want.” Mabel glanced at Rowan, then at Laurel. “It seems that you and this fair lady get along quite well, and manage to satisfy each other.” Laurel and Rowan shot questioning looks in Maynard’s direction.
“I didn’t really say anything.” Maynard was a bit flustered in spite of himself. “He just asked if you or Laurel would be interested in sex with an AI. I just said truthfully, that you both would probably not be interested because you seemed very satisfied with each other.” Maynard looked intently at both of them. “I didn’t think you’d mind that.” He paused. “After all . . . if you had ever wanted an AI bedmate, you could have created one.” 
“It’s OK.” Rowan smiled at Maynard. “I know you wouldn’t give away any family secrets.” He turned from Maynard to Mabel. “He’s right though . . . we do seem to do quite well together.” Rowan grinned at Laurel and she grinned back. “Not that we haven’t discussed it. We admit it has possibilities. But for the present, we’ll just fumble along on our own.” 
“Well . . . I guess that’s it then. I can’t think of anything else you’d want.” Mabel said a bit disheartened. “Maynard didn’t say anything improper but, by the way he answered my questions, I could tell there doesn’t seem to be anything else you need. Natural resources, you have. And soon you’ll have all the water you can use. You’re already quite well known for your research and Laurel’s books, so even my influence to make you famous with the other spacers is not needed. I guess I just don’t have anything with which to strike a deal.” Mabel rose to leave the table. 
“I’ve been able to round up a fleet of spaceships for you. Maynard assures me that the number and sizes will be more than enough mass to move your comet in time. So . . . just let me know when and where you want them and I’ll deliver a helpful fleet.” Mabel informed them.
During Mabel’s speech about the fleet he had lined up, Laurel and Rowan had been communicating in the secret fashion Maynard had observed on occasion before. They appeared to read each other’s minds and only a few gestures were needed to get an idea across to the other. He watched fascinated. 
“Wait.” Rowan spoke before Mabel was able to completely leave their table. “I think we can still strike a deal.” 
“I beg your pardon?” Mabel turned as though not believing he had heard Rowan right. 
“You do have something I want.” Rowan smiled at Mabel. 
“What? What do I have that you could possibly want?” Mabel asked puzzled.
“You have friendship.” Rowan said with a grin. “You have friendship to offer. And it would be worth it to me to give a friend the ability to taste.” He saw Mabel start to smile. “But wait . . . before you agree . . . you should know that being a friend is not always easy. Don’t answer before you’ve thought it over.” 
“You’re right, friendship is a tricky concept.” Mabel smiled at Rowan. “Why don’t we all retire to my sitting room again? We can try and hash out an acceptable deal for both of us. Shall we?” 
“Sure, that suits me.” Rowan turned to Laurel with a smile. “Come on, Laurel. Let’s go hear what the man has to offer.” 
Rowan followed Laurel as she followed Mabel and Maynard towards the edge of the Tavern. Sure enough, when Mabel got to the wall a door appeared. Just like that, they went through the door and back into Mabel’s sitting room. 

* * *

Laurel immediately went over to the fireplace and stood in front of its warmth. Maynard sat in a chair out of the way and prepared to listen and watch the proceedings carefully. This kind of exchange was just what he liked to observe. Rowan took a chair a little ways away from the fire and stretched his long legs out in front of him. 
Mabel had disappeared into an adjacent room as soon as they had entered, and Rowan waited patiently for him to return. It was only a few moments later, that Mabel took the seat opposite Rowan. When Rowan looked up as Mabel sat down, he gasped. 
Rowan couldn’t help it. Before him was a very beautiful woman. The woman had the same dark good looks as the Mabel they had already met. But on this Mabel, the dark good looks had a very feminine quality about them. Her eyes smoldered with much the same look as before, but this time aimed directly at Rowan. Mabel was clad in barely anything. Just a black wispy long bit of shimmering cloth that billowed out here and there leaving very little to the imagination. Mabel looked intently at Rowan and a smile played at the edges of her mouth. 
“I told you I make a beautiful woman.” Mabel spoke quietly. “Besides . . . I need all the help I can get in this negotiation.” She laughed, but it was a much less rough laugh this time.
“Close your mouth, dear.” Laurel grinned at Rowan. She couldn’t help but tease him. 
Rowan made a concerted effort. If he hadn’t been expecting a man to sit down, the shock would probably not have been as great. 
“Well . . . I guess you’re right.” Rowan attempted a rather weak laugh. “You’re a bit more distracting to me in your present guise.” 
“Are you still sure you don’t want to include a few nights of passion in these negotiations?” Mabel rose and twirled for Rowan’s benefit. “As you can see . . . it would be fun.” She paused in front of Rowan and smiled down at him. “I guarantee it.” 
“Yes . . . I . . . ah . . . see and I believe you. But . . . no thank you just the same.” Rowan shot a look in Laurel’s direction and she blew him a kiss. 
“My . . . but you two must be two very unusual humans.” Mabel glanced at first Laurel, and then Rowan. “Yes . . . quite unusual.” She sat down gracefully. “Shall we begin our negotiations? You said something about friendship. Please continue.” 
“Well . . . friendship involves caring about someone and helping them when you can. Basically it means having respect for someone. You’re very correct, when you say that the concept is tricky. It is. Most people never grasp the concept completely. Friendship is also a growing kind of a thing. You could offer friendship, and we could try to be friends.” Rowan glanced at Laurel for help. 
“Friendship sometimes takes years to achieve. Then again, sometimes it’s instantaneous.” Laurel glanced at Rowan, a warm glow lighting her eyes. “Like Rowan and me. We just met and ‘clicked’. Instantly we were friends forever. In our case, it was like magic.” Laurel turned her eyes towards Maynard where he sat nearby. “Other times, like with Maynard, friendship is something that grows as you get to know each other.” Laurel gazed intently into the other woman’s dark eyes. “We don’t take friendship lightly. Friendship is one of the most precious things in the world.” 
“That’s right.” Rowan agreed. “That’s why it would be worth it to me, to help a friend gain the ability to taste. Besides . . . I’m planning to publish my research and results as soon as I get the time. I’d like all Als to be able to have the ability to taste, if they want it.” 
“Rare.” Mabel spoke in a quiet voice. She didn’t say anything else for a time and the room remained in silence. “How unusual . . . humans that seem to care about AIs.” 
“They don’t just seem to care. They really do.” Maynard said from his corner of the room. “They risked their lives to rescue an AI.” 
“They did?” Mabel just sat quietly and nibbled on the end of one of her fingers. She tried to contemplate the concept of a human risking their life for an AI. “It just might be handy to have such humans as my friends.” 
“There’s something else I would like to add as a request on my side of the negotiations.” Rowan continued. 
“Well . . . what do you know? I have two things that you want.” Mabel sounded surprised. 
“Yes, I’d also like to work with you to advance AI technology even further. In our research, we’ve continually tried to advance AIs to the place where they feel themselves to be independent beings. We’d like to continue this effort, and also expand it to include helping AIs become a separate species or community. What we can do to help bring this about, I’m not sure. But . . . we’d like to try. And I’d greatly appreciate any help you could give us along those lines.” Rowan glanced towards Maynard. 
“We’ve tried our best to insure that the AIs we build have freedom of choice. We think it would be nice if more AIs could realize this freedom of choice as well. The concept of freedom of choice is very complex. We’re still attempting to upgrade and improve the technology needed to help AIs assimilate this concept.” The intensity of Rowan’s feelings shone in his eyes as he implored the beautiful woman across from him to understand. “With your contacts and knowledge, I’m sure you could help our research along tremendously.” 
“God . . . did I say unusual? You’re down right unbelievable.” Mabel stated. Her astonishment was evident in her tone of voice. “You want to help the AI community gain more freedom of choice. Now . . . that’s something I never expected to hear a human say.” 
“Well then . . . you’ve been hanging out with the wrong humans.” Rowan grinned at Mabel.
“Obviously I have.” Mabel stood up and reached out a hand towards Rowan. “You’ve got a deal.” 
Rowan was reaching out to shake Mabel’s hand, when they were interrupted by Maynard’s voice. 
“I apologize . . . for interrupting at this delicate point in your negotiations.” Maynard spoke hesitantly. “I wonder if I might put in a personal request as part of the negotiations.” He sent an apologetic look in Rowan’s direction. “I realize this is most unusual.” 
“Well . . . I’m agreeable to you making a request as part of the negotiations.” Rowan answered Maynard, and then he turned to Mabel. “Maynard will be participating in our procedure to give you the ability to taste. As far as I’m concerned, he can have a request included in the negotiations. What do you think? Do you want to consider one more additional request from our side of the negotiation table?” 
“Well . . . another request . . . I had more to bargain with than I thought.” Mabel swiveled her chair around so that she faced Maynard. “Well . . . Maynard . . . you could be an excellent resource for me. I could find out firsthand from you what it’s like to have the ability to taste. The firsthand knowledge of an AI, who has been privileged with the ability for a considerable length of time, would be valuable. This . . . in addition to your helping me acquire my ability to taste . . . might be enough. What is it that you would like to request?” 
“This is very difficult for me.” Maynard paused and looked a bit plaintively from Laurel to Rowan. “I would have preferred a chance to speak to Rowan and Laurel before making my request known. But time and circumstance doesn’t permit that.” Maynard rose and began to pace back and forth in the space located at the back of the sitting room. 
“Maynard . . . are you OK?” Laurel asked quietly. She had never seen him in quite this state before. 
“Yes . . . thank you for your concern.” Maynard smiled weakly in her direction. “I’m just concerned that I not upset you and Rowan unnecessarily. Had I more time to discuss this with you, I’m sure the shock wouldn’t be as great.” 
“Well . . . whatever it is you want to request . . . you have a right and a responsibility to do what you want. Laurel and I might be shocked as you think, but we’ll come to understand if you explain it to us.” Rowan attempted to help as best he could. 
“I must say, I’m at a loss as to how to proceed.” Maynard went and sat in the vacant chair by Rowan and Mabel. 
“Well . . . why don’t you just jump in . . . and say it . . . and see what happens.” Laurel tried to be helpful. “Sometimes . . . that’s all one can do.” 
“Well . . . in this case . . . I believe you’re right.” Maynard paused for what seemed a long time. “I’d like to change my form. I’d like the appearance of a man and I’d like Mabel to equip me with the technology and knowledge to be a bedmate.”
Rowan and Laurel exchanged quick looks. Laurel had designed Maynard to look as he did now, and it was a bit of a shock to realize he wanted to look differently. The part about wanting to become a bedmate, that was understandable when one thought about it. 
“Wow.” Laurel commented after a second or two. She and Rowan had discussed what it might be like, if Maynard ever decided to change his looks. She wanted Maynard to look the way he wanted to, it would just be a bit of a change to see him as a man. 
“I apologize again for the shock. This isn’t the way I would’ve chosen to tell you of my decision.” Maynard watched Rowan and Laurel closely. 
“Oh, you don’t need to apologize, Maynard.” Laurel went over to Maynard and took his paw, kissing his cheek. “You have a perfect right to look and be anything you want.” She smiled into his eyes. “I was just trying to picture you as a man and I think it will take me a day or two to get used to it.” 
“It’s all right, Maynard.” Rowan tried to reassure him. “We understand your need to change. We’ll be more than happy to help you in any way we can.” 
“Well . . . I must say that Maynard’s request involves what I consider my specialty.” Mabel smiled at Maynard. “You’ve come to the right place with your request. It does involve a fair amount of work on my part, though. If Rowan will provide the resources and additional help, I think I just might enjoy the challenge. It’s always inspiring to have a blank canvas on which to create. I say we still have a deal.” Mabel stood up and reached out once again to shake Rowan’s hand. 
Rowan stood up and shook Mabel’s hand solemnly. Then after a pause, he reached out and took her hand again and kissed it. 
“It was a pleasure to meet you, Mabel . . . a distinct pleasure.” Rowan sat down again and stretched out his legs. 
Mabel looked at her hand where Rowan had just kissed it. 
“Priceless.” She said sitting down again. “I might just get more benefits out of this bargain than I was aware of . . . maybe benefits much more valuable than the ability to taste.” 
Rowan and Laurel just smiled at her.
“So . . . when do you want the ability to taste?” Rowan asked Mabel, smiling at her. “Once we get back to our ship, it should only take a little while.” He glanced at Laurel. “Didn’t we update Rodjer in one afternoon?” 
“You’d do it now . . . this soon?” Mabel spoke in surprise. 
“If you want us to, we can.” Rowan assured her. 
“Well . . . of course I want that.” Mabel answered, smiling at him. “I just need a few minutes to make arrangements to leave my Tavern. I’ve always been prepared for just such a necessity. But up until now . . . I’ve never had the occasion to want to leave.” Mabel stood up and gestured towards the bar. “Please . . . help yourselves. I’ll only be gone for a few moments.” 
Mabel disappeared out the door and Rowan wandered over to the bar and requested a beer. 
“Well . . . Laurel . . . what do you think?” Rowan looked at Laurel where she stood by the fire. 
“Well . . . I hope Mabel travels with us as a man.” Laurel laughed. “I think it’s kind of hard on you when he masquerades as a woman.” 
“You think so?” Rowan laughed, catching Laurel’s eye. “Maybe you’re right. God . . . she makes a good-looking woman.” 
“Yes . . . and a good-looking man, too. I wonder how she does it.” Laurel commented. 
“Maybe . . . eventually . . . she’ll tell us.” Rowan replied quietly. 
“I want to thank you both for supporting me in my request to change.” Maynard joined the conversation. 
“Well . . . of course . . . we support you.” Laurel sounded surprised. “We want you to be whatever you want to be. Didn’t you know that?”
“I know you want me to be free to make decisions for myself . . . but I knew it would be a shock.” Maynard tried to clarify. 
“Well . . . it is a bit of a shock. But we’ll get used to it.” Laurel smiled at Maynard, hugging his small furry form. “It will be fun to help you change.” 
“What do you think of Mabel?” Rowan asked Maynard. 
“From what I’ve seen so far, with Mabel’s help we might be able to come up with some fantastic new advancements for AIs.” Maynard sounded impressed. 
“You’re right, I think.” Rowan commented. “That Tavern is outrageous.”
“Well . . . we’ll just have to wait and see.” Laurel said quietly. 
They didn’t have long to wait before Mabel returned. A few minutes later, true to her word, Mabel returned. And when the door opened, they found Mabel had returned as a man. Mabel took in their expressions. 
“You’re going to have to get used to my changes, if we’re going to be friends.” Mabel smiled at them. “I can’t have your mouths dropping open every time I change.” 
“Well . . . just give us a few more changes to get used to it.” Laurel replied with a smile. “It just takes a bit of getting used to . . . that’s all.” 
“You . . . fair lady . . . I never want you to get used to it.” Mabel smiled into Laurel’s eyes. “I always want you to take notice every time I change into a man.” 
Laurel glanced at Rowan with a helpless expression. 
“This is definitely going to take some getting used to.” Laurel spoke in a determined manner. 
“Well . . . if you’re ready?” Rowan gestured towards the door. “Let’s get a move on. We have a man here that has never tasted my super-duper burgers. We must remedy that situation as soon as possible.” 
Rowan headed for the door. Then he thought better of it, and gestured for Mabel to lead the way. One by one, they all filed out the door. Laurel and Rowan were only a little surprised when on the other side of the door, they found the empty corridor. Mabel swiftly led them back through the junctures of empty corridors until they reached Recon. 
As soon as Recon detached itself from the cluster of spaceships, Rowan and Laurel excused themselves and retired for the night behind the door Amelia had so thoughtfully prepared for their comfort. Maynard and Mabel started the evening by settling down to discuss the advances Rowan would be making to Mabel’s systems. But the discussion soon turned into Maynard relating eating experiences to Mabel. Maynard tried his best to describe in detail what tasting was like. To all concerned, the night passed very quickly.
 

## Chapter 4 — MABEL AND INDIGO




It was early afternoon of the next day, when Rowan and Laurel emerged from the back section of Recon. They found Maynard munching on tortilla chips which he generously dipped in a bowl of nacho cheese dip. Mabel seemed to be occupied with the task of enviously watching him. 
“How are you doing?” Rowan turned to address Maynard. “You look like you’re enjoying quite a snack. Having a few bits of tortilla chips with your nacho cheese, are you?” Rowan teased him. 
“Yes . . . as a matter of fact . . . I am.” Maynard answered. “How are you and Laurel this afternoon? You seem to have gotten a good deal of rest last night.” 
“We’re great, thanks.” Laurel spoke for both of them. “It was so nice to have a chance to sleep in a little.” 
“How are you this afternoon?” Rowan asked Mabel. 
“Well . . . I’m looking forward to being able to join Maynard in a similar feast soon.” Mabel looked over again at Maynard. “He seems to be enjoying himself quite thoroughly.” 
“I’m starved.” Laurel broke in. “What shall we have for breakfast? Or I guess today it’ll be lunch.” 
“First of all, I want a giant glass of orange juice with full strength vitamin C.” Rowan spoke as he made his way to the Food Dispenser. 
“That sounds good. Then . . . what about some hash browns, scrambled eggs, and some biscuits with coconut honey?” Laurel’s mouth began to water just thinking about it.
“It sounds like you have a healthy appetite.” Rowan grinned at her. 
“Well . . . one must keep one’s strength up, you know.” Laurel grinned back at Rowan and sashayed over to put her arms around him. 
“I think I want some sautéed mushrooms, too.” Rowan hugged Laurel as they made their way to the Food Dispenser. “Let’s go get lunch. My stomach is growling just thinking about it.” 
They disappeared into the middle section of Recon where the Food Dispenser was located. After a few minutes, they reappeared with plates piled high. 
“When do we get home?” Rowan asked as he began to eat his lunch. 
“We should be there in about half an hour.” Maynard said, finishing up his chips. 
“Good. We need to make sure Amelia has everything ready when we get there. We can begin working on Mabel right away.” Rowan only mumbled his words a bit as he tried to combine both eating and talking. 
“We’ve already been in communication with Amelia this morning.” Maynard replied. “Amelia and I have been going over the details of the procedure with Mabel. Everything should be all set to go when we arrive. Amelia said she would have Rodjer standing by to help as well.” 
“Great. I guess there’s nothing more we can do until we get there.” Rowan stopped talking for a bit to concentrate whole heartedly on his meal. 
Laurel sat in one of the chairs, eating her fill of her delicious lunch. She was particularly fond of biscuits dripping with coconut honey. But she felt a bit self-conscious. Mabel kept his dark eyes trained on her. She wasn’t sure if he was watching her because of her or because of the food she was eating.
“How are you today, Mabel?” Laurel smiled up at Mabel. “Do you have any idea what you want to eat for your first real meal?” 
“Well . . . I know what my first choice of a meal would consist of, pretty lady.” Mabel’s eyes took on an increasingly intense look as his gaze traveled slowly down Laurel’s body to pause at length where her shorts ended at her long tanned legs. 
“Ah . . . well . . . if your choice of a meal is something the Food Dispenser can create, I’d like to hear your ideas.” Laurel continued very carefully. 
“I do have some idea of what I’d like for that kind of a meal.” Mabel smiled at her. Then Mabel glanced at Rowan. “I’ve heard details about Rowan’s super-duper burgers. They sound very good. But Maynard says I’ll have to wait until the Sunday barbecue to taste them. So . . . I guess my second . . .” Mabel grinned over at Laurel. “Make that my third choice . . . would have to be a sampling of Maynard’s favorites. Nacho cheese with chips . . . macaroni and cheese . . . cheese enchiladas . . . and grilled cheese sandwiches.” 
“That sounds good . . . if you happen to like cheese.” Rowan glanced at Maynard and laughed. “But I think we can do better than that. If you don’t happen to end up liking cheese as much as Maynard does, you might not be suitably impressed.” 
“How about having beer and pizza?” Laurel suggested around a bite of biscuit.
“Now, that’s a great idea.” Rowan loved beer. He had only just recently developed the new formula for the Food Dispenser. “What do you think of Laurel’s suggestion, Maynard?” 
“That would be good.” Maynard’s one other food passion besides cheese, just so happened to be beer. And he was quite impressed with Rowan’s latest effort in beer making. “It sounds great as long as my pizza can have extra cheese on it.” 
“Sure.” Rowan smiled at Maynard. “Triple extra cheese . . . if you want.” 
“It sounds good to me, too.” Mabel joined in. “I’ve heard many references about pizza which lead me to believe it would taste good. And the pizza parlor is a very popular choice at my Tavern. I’d like to try it.” 
“OK. Tonight when we get you all fixed up, it will be pizza and beer all around.” Rowan glanced at Maynard. “Can you make sure that everyone is notified? Tonight we’re going to have a pizza party.” 
“Why don’t we have the pizza party at the Dance Floor in the meadow?” Laurel added. “We could eat pizza, drink beer, and dance the night away.” 
“That’s a great idea.” Rowan leaned over and kissed Laurel on the top of her head. Her mouth was still busy eating. “You’re just full of good ideas today.” 
“Who me?” Laurel grinned up at Rowan. “I just want to get you out on the dance floor for a few slow dances.” 
“Yes . . . I know.” Rowan’s eyes twinkled at the corners as he smiled down at Laurel. “You just want us to get all heated up out on the dance floor, and then you’ll have your way with me. I know you.”
