“OK.” Mabel stood up also, moving to stand very close to Maynard. “But . . . first . . . could I have a kiss goodbye?” She teased him standing up on tiptoe and reaching up to kiss him, her breasts brushing against his chest. 
“Could I interest you in more . . . than a kiss goodbye?” Maynard found himself saying. “No . . . never mind . . . I can’t do that. I’ve got a limited amount of time and I must talk to you as soon as possible.” He said haltingly, stepping away from Mabel. “This is difficult. Is it always this difficult?” 
“Yes.” Mabel smiled. “But . . . I’m glad it’s difficult. Otherwise . . . I’d be losing my charms. So . . . off with you. I’ll meet you in an hour.” 
Maynard turned and started across the grass as Mabel wandered off down the beach. He didn’t know how Mabel changed, but it was clear she didn’t want anyone around when she did it. Once again Maynard found himself hurrying across the ship to Lab. He hoped this time he’d have as much success with Mabel as he had last time with Amelia. 

* * *

When Mabel showed up in Lab an hour later, he was a man. He found Maynard deep in conversation with either himself or Amelia, Mabel didn’t know which. All Mabel knew was that as he opened the door, he heard voices which suddenly stopped. And there was no one but Maynard in the room. 
“Talking to yourself, are you?” Mabel asked with a grin. 
“No. Amelia and I were just discussing a future project. But she understood I would need to talk to you as soon as you arrived.” Maynard answered quietly.
“What do you want to talk to me about?” Mabel made his way to the couch and sat down stretching his elegantly clad legs out in front of him. “It must be important to take you away from your other projects.” 
“Yes, it’s very important.” Maynard began to pace back and forth. “I don’t have much time, so I’m going to have to come straight to the point and hope you understand.”
“OK, fire away.” Mabel replied. 
“I’d like your help in making Amelia an independent person. I’d like your help in achieving for her an independent existence, much like the one you’ve managed to acquire for yourself. Amelia is agreeable to this plan and I’m sure Rowan will provide the raw materials. All I need from you is your expertise and knowledge. It’s paramount if we’re to succeed quickly. Quite possibly it’s necessary if we’re to succeed at all.”
“You’re asking for quite a bit.” Mabel’s voice grew serious. 
“I realize that.” Maynard answered. “But . . . I have strong and compelling reasons that may just convince you to help us.” He paused in his pacing and sat down facing Mabel. 
“First . . . you’ve done an excellent job in helping to turn me into a bedmate. But as a result of your excellent job, I’m faced with a problem.” Maynard looked closely at Mabel’s face, searching for some sign that he was being understood. 
“I have no sex partner. You’ll be returning to your Tavern. The women on board this ship, if they had wanted a bedmate, would’ve made one long ago. Amelia has agreed to be my sex partner should we succeed in making her an independent person.” Maynard continued earnestly. 
“Second . . . Amelia could greatly advance herself by being able to move independently of the ship.” Maynard continued to number off his reasons. “You’d be helping in the advancement of another AI.” 
“Third . . . you’d be helping in the furthering of Amelia’s and my research into AI and human relationships. As an AI couple, we’ll learn much more information about AIs. Much of what we learn will also be able to be used to further the understanding of humans as well.” Maynard paused before continuing. 
“Fourth . . . as far as we can discover, you’re the only being so far able to accomplish the feat of transferring a ship-wide management consciousness into an independent body. If there was a second like-being around, this person of similar existence would no doubt be able to help you by gaining information. From their experiences encountered while living as you do, they’d be able to formulate helpful updates that would benefit you both.” Maynard paused to clarify his words. “In other words, if Amelia were living like you are now, she may gain insights that would improve your existence.” 
“Fifth . . . Amelia and I are prepared to share with you any information and fame we might get as a result of the data we collect as sex partners. Also any information or insights gained as a result of Amelia’s living an independent existence will be shared with you as well.” Maynard gazed intently into Mabel’s face. 
“And sixth . . . you can’t leave me here without a sex partner. You’ve helped to make me want sex this much. Now . . . I’m asking for your help in creating an outlet for that need you have created in me.” 
“Ah . . .” Mabel leaned back against the couch cushions and regarded Maynard seriously. “I think you got me with that sixth reason. Up until then, I was swaying a bit. All your reasons carry a lot of weight. But . . . you’re asking a lot of me . . . quite a lot.” Then a slow smile spread across Mabel’s face. “But that sixth reason . . . you knew that would get to me . . . because it’s tied into our other bargain to make you a bedmate. How could I have succeeded in making you a bedmate . . . if you had no one to bed?” 
“So . . . will you help us?” Maynard realized that, had he breath to hold, he would have been holding it. 
“Yes, I’ll help.” Mabel stood up. “But . . . only with very strict conditions that you and Amelia and Rowan and Laurel will have to agree to uphold.” He saw Maynard’s face, and laughed. “Don’t worry . . . I’m sure you’ll all agree they’re fair conditions. So . . . go and round up Rowan and Laurel. We don’t have any time to waste. I need to get back to my Tavern.” 
“Amelia . . . did you hear?” Maynard asked in an excited voice. 
“Yes.” Amelia answered. “I’d like to thank you, Mabel. You’re quite generous. I’ll inform Rowan and Laurel that we need them in Lab immediately.” 
“Well . . . they should be here soon.” Maynard commented. “Is there anything we can do in the meantime?” 
“Yes. I need to see if you have the necessary raw materials on board. I’d also like something to eat. It may be hours before I’ll get time to sample food again once we get started. Also is there any word on when Rowan is going to attempt to move the comet?” Mabel asked.
“Amelia said that Rowan has decided to move the comet tomorrow. She has the sling constructed and everything should be ready. She has put out the word and, just as you promised, everyone has agreed to show up here tomorrow.” Maynard filled Mabel in on the latest about the comet. 
“Good. That means my Tavern will be here about the time I’ve completed all the work on Amelia. But we’re going to have to work day and night until then. Of course . . . when I first shifted into an independent body . . . it took much longer than it will take Amelia. I’ve managed to learn a thing or two since then.” Mabel commented.
Mabel sat down at one of the workstations and began to go over the list of raw materials he would need. Maynard brought over some cheese enchiladas for both of them. He could hardly believe the good fortune of Mabel’s decision to help them. He’d have to thank Amelia later for suggesting reason number six. 


## Chapter 6 — AMELIA




Rowan sat slumped over his umpteenth cup of coffee at the edge of a Lab worktable. Laurel was wrapped in a blanket on the couch, trying to stay awake as the night hours crept slowly by. 
When Rowan and Laurel had arrived at Lab about noon the day before, they had been swept up in the plans and excitement that had permeated the atmosphere in Lab. Maynard had excitedly laid out his and Amelia’s plans. Rowan and Laurel had been consecutively stunned, amazed, and delighted at the changes that awaited Amelia. The technical advances involved in the transfer were mind-boggling. Rowan had agreed quite generously to supply the raw materials, just as Maynard and Amelia had hoped he would. 
Mabel’s conditions which all four of them had to agree to before they began, were eminently fair. The conditions involved Mabel retaining silent ownership of the technical processes involved and the request that Amelia not make public her abilities at the present time. Apparently Mabel wasn’t ready yet to make his type of existence known publicly. 
The previous afternoon and evening had been filled with complicated talk and much discussion. Amelia made all the decisions regarding the form and appearance she would eventually have. Then with the nanotechnology machines constructing the parts Amelia needed out of the raw materials on board, the action turned to the complicated procedure involved in the actual transfer. Here Rowan and Maynard stood back, watching fascinated as Mabel worked his magic. Amelia seemed to understand what was expected of her and was able to follow Mabel’s requests. Laurel watched and observed the happenings, sometimes completely lost as the technical expertise exceeded her knowledge. But whenever this happened, someone would always graciously put the general gist of what was happening into language she could understand. 
Now as the last of the long night hours dwindled into dawn, exhaustion had set in, at least for the humans involved in the project. Maynard and Mabel were bent over a female form laid out on one of the worktables. Tirelessly they were making the final adjustments to the hardware that would contain Amelia when the transfer was complete.
“I’m going to curl up with Laurel and take a bit of a nap.” Rowan spoke from behind Maynard, where he had come to take a final look before getting a bit of a well-deserved rest. Rowan hated to miss any of the technological wizardry, and he had managed to stay awake for the important and complicated technical procedures involved. Now Maynard and Mabel were finishing up the bedmate and appearance features. 
“Please wake me if I’m still sleeping when Amelia is ready to transfer.” Rowan said stifling a yawn behind his hand. 
“OK.” Maynard looked back over his shoulder at Rowan. “Catch up on your rest a bit. We still have to get the comet moved later on today.” 
“Yes. It seems like a body needs to be an AI just to keep up with what’s happening around here these days.” Rowan smiled. “Thank goodness I have you, Maynard, to keep everything going while I knock off for a bit. Good luck.” Rowan spoke this last over his shoulder as he made his way through the construction debris. 
“Can I join you?” Rowan whispered as he climbed under the blanket with Laurel. 
“Uh, huh . . . just a sec . . . I’ll make room.” Laurel murmured sleepily. 
Laurel scrunched over and together she and Rowan managed to get semi-comfortable on the couch. Intertwined and snuggled close, they sank quickly into sleep. They were so exhausted that they didn’t even notice the relative discomfort of the couch. For as comfortable as the couch might be, it was still no where near as comfortable as the bed in their dome. 
It was mid-morning when Maynard gently awakened Rowan and Laurel. He had brought over two cups of very strong coffee and some sweet rolls, so they could jump start their systems. 
“It’s time to wake up.” Maynard spoke gently as he put their breakfast down on the table by the couch. 
Laurel was the first to wake up, for she was a much lighter sleeper than Rowan. But after a few nudges and some quite loud murmurs in his ear, she managed to wake Rowan as well. 
“Thanks for breakfast, Maynard.” Laurel said as she reached for a steaming cup of coffee. “How’s it going?” 
“Mabel says we’re nearly ready for Amelia to begin her transfer.” Maynard motioned to the far side of Lab. “I thought I should wake you though, to give you a chance to wake up before she begins her transfer. I know you wouldn’t want to miss anything at this stage.”
“Thanks.” Rowan spoke with a blurry voice as he reached for a sweet roll. “What time is it, anyway?” 
“It’s almost 11:00 a.m.” Maynard answered. “Mabel is just finishing up the last updates to Amelia’s system. The transfer should take place soon.” 
Rowan picked up his cup of coffee and, with his partially eaten sweet roll in his hand, he made his way through Lab to see what changes had taken place while he was asleep. Laurel sipped the last of her coffee, and then grabbed the plate of sweet rolls and followed in his wake. Only managing to trip once or twice on the blanket wrapped around her, she made her way slowly through the obstacle course. 
“Amelia chose a great combination for her appearance.” Laurel commented, when she saw the body that had been constructed for Amelia. “It’s certainly beautiful.”
“Yes.” Rowan agreed as he gazed at Amelia’s future body. 
Amelia had chosen a tall female body of slight proportions and light complexion. Long flaming red hair fell in a bit of disarray around the beautiful freckled face. As yet, the body remained unclothed. It was plain to see that Amelia had chosen small pert breasts to compliment her slight body proportions. They couldn’t tell the color Amelia had chosen for her eyes as the eyes of the body were closed. 
“She chose a beautiful combination of features.” Rowan turned to Maynard as he came over to where Rowan and Laurel stood admiring the night’s work. 
“Yes.” Maynard gazed down at the body Amelia would soon inhabit. “I’m delighted with her choices.” A strange look came into Maynard’s eyes. “She chose very well.” 
“Well . . . we’re as ready as we’ll ever be to transfer Amelia.” Mabel joined them from the workstation where he had been occupied ever since Rowan and Laurel had awakened.
“Amelia.” Maynard spoke quietly. “Are you ready?” 
“Yes.” Amelia answered in a voice that held only a faint touch of excitement. They could tell that she was attempting to be as professional as possible about this.
“OK.” Mabel spoke up. “We went over this whole procedure earlier this morning. So . . . you just begin the sequence and I’ll monitor your progress at the workstation.”
Mabel went back to his place at the workstation, intently watching the changes taking place on the display screen. 
Silence filled the room. Rowan and Laurel and Maynard stood silently, watching the female form lying on the worktable. If there had been a clock in the room, they could have heard it ticking. No one moved. No one spoke. It appeared as though no one in the room was even breathing. And the moments passed, one after the other, stretching into the kind of event where time seems to stand still even as it continues to go on forever. 
“Well . . . she made it.” Mabel broke the long silence. “According to my data, she has transferred and should be all right.” Mabel came over to join the small group silently gazing at the body on the worktable. 
“We just need to give Amelia a few minutes to adjust to her new environment. At first, it’s a bit of a shock. I remember it well.” Mabel continued to speak. “When I went over the procedure with Amelia, I told her to take as much time as she needed to adjust.”
“Is there anything we can do to help her?” Rowan asked quietly.
“As I told Maynard this morning when we were going over the procedure, there’s nothing to be done. As long as the display screen shows she made it, we just wait while she adjusts. It shouldn’t be too much longer.” Mabel tried to reassure them. “Amelia just needs a bit of time to adjust to her new body. She also has to run a lengthy series of internal tests I’ve given her.” 
All eyes remained motionlessly focused on the form on the worktable. Once again, time did its slow march into eternity. Laurel moved once to adjust the blanket more snugly around herself, but her eyes never left the form laid out before them. 
Eventually their wait was rewarded. The fingers of one hand and then the other began to move slowly. Then the eyes opened. And the intelligent being they had known as Amelia gazed out at them. 
She had brilliant light green eyes, eyes that could’ve been carbon copies of Maynard’s. Her eyes caught and seemed to hold the brilliance of shallow tropical waters on the sun-drenched shore of paradise. The uncommonly beautiful eyes blinked twice above the freckle-covered nose and a small smile began to form on the beautifully sculptured face. 
“So . . . are you all just going to stand around and stare at a lady at her most vulnerable moment?” Amelia teased them with a smile. She slowly sat up and looked around the room. 
“Such freedom, it’s even more than I expected.” Amelia paused. “It’s strange to be inside something I’ve only viewed from the outside. It seems I can view this room from the inside as you do, and I can also simultaneously view it from outside looking in . . . just like I always have.” 
Slowly Amelia climbed off the table and walked to the window. After gazing for a few silent moments at the grounds visible though the window, she turned and regarded the group still staring at her. 
“It’s amazing . . . total freedom . . . of a kind I’ve never experienced before.” A delicate frown momentarily lined her forehead. “No . . . not total freedom. A better way to describe it would be freedom of movement. Actually I’m quite a bit more condensed in this version of myself. While I was inside the ship, I had numerous outlets and pathways. Now even though I still have those, they’re more distant.” Amelia walked slowly back to the group watching her, stopping in front of Mabel. 
“It’s like you said, the parts of me that manage the ship are distant . . . like primitive parts of my brain. They’ll only make themselves felt in my consciousness, when a problem occurs that they can’t handle . . . much like before the transfer . . . only much more distant.” Amelia smiled at Mabel. “Like you tried to explain to me last night, most of my immediate consciousness is taken up with the here and now . . . what I see and do and feel.  It’s marvelous to have all this new information.” 
Amelia stopped talking, putting up a slim hand to brush a bit of red hair from her forehead. Then with a very serious look on her face, she moved closer to Mabel. She kissed him lightly. 
“Thank you.” Amelia stood back, a warm glow lighting her eyes. “You’ve given me a tremendous gift. I’ll be forever grateful.”
“You’re entirely welcome.” Mabel bowed gallantly in Amelia’s direction. “It’s my distinct pleasure to be able to help a ship become a lady. May you sail through life as a lady . . . the way you have sailed through space as a ship.” 
“Why . . . thank you.” Amelia smiled up into Mabel’s eyes. “What a beautiful thought.” 
“What about me?” Maynard spoke up, a smile tugging at his mouth. “Don’t I get a ‘thank you’ as well?” 
“You get more than a ‘thank you’.” Amelia walked towards Maynard, light dancing in her eyes. “You get me . . . whenever you want me.” 
Maynard reached out and brought Amelia close. Folding his arms tightly around her, Maynard kissed the lips that a few moments before had been unmoving. Now not only did those lips move, but in a delicate way they strove to transmit to him her happiness. 
“Well . . . that’s my kind of ‘thank you’.” Maynard commented after he released Amelia from his embrace. He glanced at the others gathered around them. 
“I’d also like to add my own ‘thank you’ to you all as well. You’ve all been most generous with your time, your resources, and your knowledge. I’m very grateful. You’ve helped Amelia accomplish something that is so far reaching in its entirety that I can only begin to fathom what it will eventually mean.” 
“I must thank you as well.” Amelia moved towards Rowan. “You created me in such a way, that I’m now able to possess such knowledge and power and freedom. I’ll be forever in your debt.” Amelia reached out and touched Rowan’s cheek. “This is the first time I’ve ever touched a human.” She paused. “So much heart and soul in such a fragile container . . . like touching whisper-thin crystal.” Amelia reached up and kissed him. “How can I ever show you how grateful I am to have had the luck to be created by you?”
“And you, Laurel . . .” Amelia turned in Laurel’s direction. “You’re a very special lady. I’ve had the privilege of knowing you and Rowan for a good many years. You’re both very special. The freedoms you give each other and encourage in each other are extended to your AIs as well.” Amelia moved to stand by Laurel. “I’m grateful to you for sharing Rowan with me. You have a very generous nature.” She kissed Laurel’s cheek and received a hug in return. 
“I realize you’re an AI that’s just been given a tremendous new lease on life. But you’re also a woman. As such, you’ve joined the ranks of the other females on board. I believe they would not only like to meet you, but I think Juniper has come up with some clothes that you might like.” Laurel said and glanced around at the admiring glances from the males present that fell on Amelia’s form. “I think we should escape for a few moments to Juniper’s dome and get you fashionably attired. Then I think a bit of a celebration is in order.” Laurel glanced around the room. “I know we have to move the comet today . . . but we could have a celebration later.” 
“We’re not due to move the comet until about 3:00 p.m. this afternoon.” Amelia put in before anyone else could answer. “So I think I’ll take you up on that offer of clothes.” She grinned at Laurel. “You know . . . I can almost feel their gazes warming my skin.” 
“No doubt.” Laurel spoke as she transferred the blanket from her shoulders to Amelia’s. 
The sun was up far enough to keep her warm enough with just her shorts and t-shirt. And covering up Amelia was probably the only way the men would let Amelia out of their sight. Laurel was amazed at the gentlemanly way their eyes had tried to stay on Amelia’s face, only occasionally drifting downwards. 

* * *

Leaving the men to straighten up Lab and return it to its proper orderliness, Laurel and Amelia made their way quickly to Juniper’s dome. Amelia’s comments about the scenery they passed and the fragrances she smelled, filled their conversation with cries of surprise and pleasure. Willow and Juniper waited for them in Juniper’s dome, amid a scrambled variety of samples of clothing styles. Some Juniper had designed especially in honor of Amelia and some were favorites of the other women on board.
Following the arrival of Laurel and Amelia, the room was filled with exclamations of delight at the way Amelia looked. There was also excitement at having yet another female on board. Amelia tried on a variety of clothes. Eventually she came to something that Laurel had added to the display, an army-surplus-style flight suit. Complete with zippers, epaulets, and pockets. Amelia took an instant liking to it.
Immediately after witnessing Amelia’s expressed delight in the flight suit, Juniper went into overdrive. With great delight and enthusiasm, she used her expertise to help Amelia fashion a whole wardrobe around the same type of clothing. Everyone present gave their suggestions and helpful comments, with Amelia making all the final decisions. Soon a distant part of Amelia’s management system was overseeing the nanotechnology machines as they constructed her chosen wardrobe. 
While they awaited the soon-to-be dispensed clothing, the women sat in a haphazard circle on the floor. Amid the scattered array of discarded clothing samples, they talked just as any group of women talk when they know each other well. For even if Amelia’s appearance and presence among them was new, they had all still known each other for years. 
Amelia had dozens of questions on all sorts of topics, and each one was gladly answered to the best of the ability of those present. A good many of the questions centered on men and sex, since Amelia had been outfitted to be a bedmate. After all, one of the purposes for taking on her present form was to be Maynard’s bedmate. Personal stories and opinions and advice were dispensed by those present in the most helpful manner. 
They were all surprised, when they realized the time had passed so quickly. It seemed instantly to be almost 3:00 p.m. and time to move the comet. Amelia’s new clothes waited in the Dispenser for her, where they had long ago been dispensed unnoticed. 
“We’ll have a celebration tonight.” Laurel assured Amelia. “That way we can celebrate the start of the comet moving as well.” 
“I best get dressed and go attend to ship’s duties.” Amelia murmured as she jumped up and hurriedly selected something to wear. While quickly dressing, she paused to smile at them all. “It’s been great fun talking. Let’s do it again soon. And thanks so much for the clothes. They’re great.” 
“Sure.” The others assured her as she ran out the door. “Anytime.” 
Laurel stayed to help Willow and Juniper put the cyclone-hit dome back to rights. With all three of them working, it didn’t take long. Mostly they just had to take armfuls of the unwanted items and put them in the Recycle Slot. They each saved out a few items to either return or add to their own wardrobes. Soon they had completed their task and were hurrying down the path to Group. A communications system had been set up there to coordinate the activities between their ship and the others waiting to help move the comet. 

* * *

When Amelia arrived at Group, she found Rowan and Mabel and Maynard and Rodjer had already arrived. Having completed the cleanup of Lab, they were relaxing around the conversation pit with mugs of foamy beer. They were just commenting that it was almost time to contact the ships in standing orbit around them and begin the complicated procedure to move the comet, when Amelia entered. 
Amelia was surprised at the effect her entrance had on them. She was used to joining any gathering, her speaking presence causing no visible reactions from those present. Even before she spoke, Amelia was aware that merely her presence impacted them and each to a different degree. She could see it in their eyes. 
“Hello.” Amelia said as she entered Group. 
She looked stunning in her earth-green flight suit. The color went well with her hair and eyes, and the style had a certain feminine authority about it. The fact that she was a woman wearing male-type clothing, seemed to highlight her femininity .Of course the fact that her flight suit had been designed to accentuate and adhere to every curve, was beside the point. The effect was breathtaking. The men couldn’t help but show their appreciation for her appearance. 
“Hello.” Rowan was the first to answer. “You look lovely. I see that Juniper has lived up to her reputation yet again.” 
“Yes.” Amelia moved towards the conversation pit. “Thanks to the women on board, I now have a great supply of clothing. They were very generous with their suggestions and they all have exceptional good taste.” 
“Yes . . . we can see that.” Maynard said an appreciative glint in his eye. “You look great. Are you ready to move the comet? Everyone is in position and standing by.” 
“Then . . . let’s begin.” Amelia went to one of the workstations and began the delicate procedures necessary to coordinate the spreading of the sling and the attachment of it to the numerous ships, turning them all into a fleet of comet movers. 
Internally, parts of Amelia’s management systems on the ship directed some of the proceedings. While externally, by way of speech and the display screen, Amelia also controlled others. It felt good and secure to once again be totally absorbed with the running of the ship. The organization of events such as this had been her responsibility for so many years. There were even moments when Amelia wasn’t aware that she had changed at all. Her involvement in the proceedings was so great. 
When all the ships were in position and everything was organized and on line, it was really quite simple. The giant sling cradled the comet with long attachments coming off the sling at various places attached to the various ships. The fleet of ships, including theirs and Mabel’s Tavern, each used their mass to pull in unison. 
Once Amelia had all the ships aligned and attached and pulling in unison, there was nothing more to do. Each ship’s system kept that particular ship in its designated place in the formation moving at the designated speed towards the comet’s new orbit location. Any variations to the complex formula were caught early and realigned. There was nothing more to be done for the next twenty-four hours or so. Each of the individual ship’s systems did the rest, with one of Amelia’s coordination systems overseeing everything. 
In the first few hours after the move began, there were many communications back and forth between the ships. Various checks and tests were run and confirmed. Mabel announced the celebration that would commence on board the Tavern when the moving job was complete and each ship had had a chance to get their water. But as the hours begin to drag by, each ship settled down to pass the time until the job was done.
Amelia ran through a series of checks to make sure that everything was running smoothly. The United Earth Governments could legally do nothing as they moved their comet, but she was sure they wouldn’t be happy about it. 
When Amelia was satisfied that she had done all she could, she instigated a special system that Mabel had given her. It was the system Mabel used when he had left his Tavern. It was a very special system that enabled Amelia to leave all the systems on board the ship in the charge of a sort of ghost of herself. It enabled Amelia to have freedom from the management duties of the ship. The ghost would notify her if she was needed. In the meantime, she was free to explore her new surroundings without any awareness of the ship’s processes. 
“Well . . . that does it.” Amelia turned to the group still gathered in the “pit”. “Everything is set and running. There’s nothing more to be done until we reach the comet’s new orbit destination tomorrow night sometime.” She stood up and smoothed her flight suit. The fabric felt strange but good against her skin, like a silky breeze swishing this way and that. 
Amelia joined the others in the “pit”. Sitting on a cushion on the floor, she wrapped her arms around her knees. 
“So . . . we’re actually moving our comet to safety.” Rowan commented. “Good work.” 
“Shall we have a celebration?” Laurel asked quietly. “I say, we have a party in Amelia’s honor.” 
“That sounds great.” Rowan answered. “How about it, Amelia . . . do you feel like celebrating?” 
“Well . . . I do have some taste buds I haven’t had a chance to try out yet. And I haven’t danced yet. And I haven’t . . .” Amelia glanced in Maynard’s direction. “There are so many things I can do now that I haven’t done before, it’s hard to know where to start.” 
“Well . . .” Rowan said, glancing out the window at the level of the sun. “It looks to me like it’s close to dinner time. What if we start there?” 
Many cries of “yes” and “I’m starved” filled the room. Then a stream of suggestions cascaded towards Rowan. 
“Wait a minute.” Rowan put up a hand to stop the verbal bombardment. “Why don’t we have my super-duper burgers at a beach barbecue tomorrow at noon? That will give Mabel a chance to taste them before he goes.” Rowan smiled. “We missed our regular Sunday barbecue today . . . but we’ll have it tomorrow. How would that be?” 
Rowan got ear-splitting answers in the affirmative. Everyone loved his burgers and he figured they also liked the idea that even though they’d missed their day at the beach today, they’d be able to go tomorrow.

It was fascinating to be so closely associated with a person, that you knew what they were thinking. And he had a feeling that this was only the tip of the iceberg. 
Loading up his plate with food, Maynard sprinkled a couple extra handfuls of grated cheese on top. He watched as Amelia tasted bits of this and that.

“Well . . . you’re welcome.” Rowan answered with a smile. “But . . . if you think this meal tastes good . . . just wait until you taste my burgers.”

Amelia tried her hand at dancing. She found she enjoyed it. In her new form she found she particularly enjoyed motion, creating motion. No one seemed to notice when Maynard eventually led her from the dance floor and towards the door. He didn’t say a word. There was no need.

I think that at the very first when Mabel began with me, she just got me excited and let my reactions take over. I’d forget about trying to teach you anything.” His eyes took on a bright twinkle. “I’d just . . . ah . . . request some music and get us something to eat and talk a bit and have some fun. I’ve noticed that Rowan and Laurel are almost always communicating back and forth in sexual ways. They got caught up in analyzing the details of what the sleep program would need to accomplish and what technical capabilities were involved. Before they knew it, dawn began to brighten the sky. As the streaks of light came in through the window and lit on Amelia’s face and worked their way down her body, Maynard reached out explaining that there was just one more thing he wanted to teach her before they got out of bed.
 

## Chapter 7 — INEVITABLE OUTCOME




Morning sunlight glinted off the ocean swells. Maynard was carefully holding Amelia as she trustingly continued to learn to float. She was getting very good at it. Maynard just barely held his hands against her back and yelled encouragement. He was just deciding to teach her the next step to actual swimming, when shouts from the beach alerted him to the fact that they no longer had the beach to themselves. 
Glancing up, Maynard could just make out the forms of Laurel and Rowan walking down the beach from the Beach Shack. They were loaded down with barbecue supplies. Trailing behind with more food supplies and beach gear were Juniper and Rodjer. Maynard waved an arm in their general direction and gently pulled Amelia upright.

“How about if we come out here tonight . . . and see if we can make a few waves of our own?” Amelia smiled at Maynard, her eyes sending out sparks. 
“Now . . . that’s a splendid idea.” Maynard grinned back at her, reaching out to draw her close. 
Amelia put her legs up around his waist and put her arms around his neck. She floated there in his arms, looking up into his sparkling green eyes. 
“Oh . . . I have lots of good ideas.” Amelia kissed his neck and hugged him tight.
