I cared a great deal about you when you were a part of the ship. Now that you’re a beautiful woman . . .” A grin tugged at the corners of his mouth. “I can’t help but find you very attractive.” 
“And . . . the fact that you chose to be a red head.” Laurel grinned. “He likes red heads.” 
“Oh . . . he does, does he?” Amelia grinned at Rowan. “Now . . . that’s an interesting thing to know. No wonder you’re having a hard time.” And groans filled the air. 
“Sorry.” Amelia acknowledged. “You know what I mean.” 
“Well . . . I understand that Amelia’s change has given Rowan a bit of a difficult time, but Laurel said that both our changes have affected you both. Does that mean my change has affected Laurel in some way, too?” Maynard wondered aloud. 
“Well . . . I guess it’s my turn now.” Laurel spoke up. “Amelia . . . could you help me out here when you can? You might be able to help me make him understand.” 
“Oh . . . I think I know already.” Amelia smiled in her direction. 
“Well . . . will someone please tell me?” Maynard asked exasperatedly. 
“Please . . . go ahead.” Laurel gestured to Amelia. “You fill him in.” 
“Well . . . if I’m right . . . Laurel created a furry soft teddy bear with all the characteristics she valued. Much like Rowan did with me. She had this teddy bear around her constantly and she grew to care a great deal about him.” Amelia glanced at Laurel. “How am I doing so far?” 
“Excellent.” Laurel smiled at her. “Right on the money. And I thank you for telling him . . . I find it all a bit embarrassing.” 
“Yes . . . I think I understand.” Amelia smiled at her. “Well . . . to continue. Laurel grew to care about this teddy bear . . . even possibly to love him as a person loves a very good friend. Then . . .” Amelia turned to Maynard. “The teddy bear turned overnight into a gorgeous man. It’s very likely that the combination of features you picked for yourself, are also very attractive to her . . . just as my features are attractive to Rowan.” Amelia paused and glanced at Laurel. Laurel just barely nodded her head “yes”. 
“So . . . now . . . instead of a small furry lovable teddy bear to care about . . . Laurel gets to keep company with a gorgeous, sexy man . . . whose looks she finds very attractive.” Amelia smiled at Laurel and Laurel grinned back. 
“Now . . . if I’m right . . . when Laurel reaches out to kiss the cheek of the Maynard she used to know . . . she finds herself kissing the cheek of an almost irresistibly attractive man. When she hugs the Maynard she used to know like she used to all the time . . . instead of the small furry arms of a teddy bear . . . she feels the muscles and warmth of the arms of a man.” Amelia paused, looking intently at Laurel. “And . . . I’d venture to guess . . . that with this attractive looking man having the consideration and caring towards her that the old Maynard did . . . he would be even more attractive to her. So . . . this man is not only attractive to her because of his looks . . . but also because of his caring nature towards her and hers towards him.” Amelia paused again, watching Laurel’s face. “How’d I do?”
“Right on the mark.” Laurel answered quietly, looking at her gratefully. “And . . . thank you for explaining it. It would’ve been very difficult for me.” 
Laurel couldn’t bring herself to look at Maynard. She turned and watched the waterfall where it joined the pool. The resulting mist made some of the most beautiful rainbows she had ever seen. And she had always felt that rainbows contained a bit of magic. Laurel felt Maynard’s presence next to her, and she still didn’t dare turn to look at him. She just couldn’t. She just continued to watch the spray of rainbows and wish that she could magically be somewhere else, anywhere else. 
“I didn’t realize.” Maynard spoke quietly from beside her. “I knew my change would be the most difficult for you. But I thought it was a change you could just get used to . . . like you said.” Maynard reached out and took one of Laurel’s hands in both of his. “I had no idea that my change would affect you in this way.” 
Laurel swallowed with difficulty and remained silent. She didn’t look at Maynard. She didn’t move. She didn’t think she was even breathing. 
“Laurel.” Rowan’s voice broke into her discomfort. “I think I’ll take Amelia and go over to the Dance Floor for some drinks. After that walk, a drink would be nice. Or . . . would you rather I stayed here and waited for you to join me?” 
Laurel breathed a sigh of relief, glancing carefully in Rowan’s direction. If she was going to be embarrassed, she wanted as few people as possible to see. 
“No . . . you and Amelia go ahead.” Laurel managed to say, giving Rowan a small attempt at a smile. “And . . . thank you.” She added under her breath, but Rowan heard.
Laurel heard Rowan ask Amelia if she’d like something to drink. She heard Amelia answer in the affirmative and she heard their voices fade as they made their way over to the Dance Floor Food Dispenser. She went back to watching the rainbows, while her hand grew more and more moist in Maynard’s grasp. 
“Laurel.” Maynard spoke after a bit. “Aren’t you going to talk to me?” 
Laurel held her breath and waited. She didn’t want to say or do anything. She just couldn’t. 
“Laurel.” Maynard released her hand to reach up with one of his hands to touch her chin and, with dread, Laurel realized that he was going to turn her head to face him.
“No.” Laurel managed to stammer out. 
“Laurel . . . you can’t just sit here forever . . . not looking at me . . . and not talking to me.” Maynard spoke patiently from her side as he brought his hand away from her face.
Laurel didn’t speak or move. Moments passed and no one said a word. Then Laurel heard a bit of a sound far off, like a fish jumping out of the water. Before she could realize what had happened, Maynard’s face popped up out of the water in front of her. Instantly, but a bit too late, Laurel realized the sound she had heard was Maynard diving into the pool. 
Instinctively Laurel jerked her gaze away from the pool and turned to face sideways. Pulling her knees up to her chest, she sat with her chin on her knees, her eyes closed. She could hear Maynard swimming over to the boulder on which she sat and she knew she should just leave. But she couldn’t do that either. 
“Laurel.” Maynard spoke from just below her, where he clung to the boulder she sat on. “Please . . . talk to me. I’m getting a bit desperate.” He said in a hesitant voice. “I’ve even had to resort to diving into the pool . . . with all my clothes on.” 
Laurel turned to look, before she realized what she was doing. Sure enough, clinging to Maynard’s broad shoulders was his t-shirt. 
“Why’d you jump into the pool with your clothes on?” Laurel asked him instinctively. 
“Well . . . you wouldn’t look away from the pool. You wouldn’t let me turn your head to look at me. So . . . I thought I’d just dive into the pool and surface right in front of your eyes . . . that way you’d have to look at me.” A small grin showed just barely in Maynard’s eyes. “And . . . I couldn’t very well take off my clothes to dive into the pool. Not when I just found out that my changes seem to be upsetting you. So . . .” He gestured around himself. “I had no choice . . . but to dive in with all my clothes on.” 
Laurel didn’t know whether to laugh or cry. Finally her sense of humor won out and she laughed. Admittedly, it was a tiny laugh. But it was still a laugh, and Maynard seized on the possibility. With an arm like lightening, he reached out and caught her leg. And slowly and effortlessly, Maynard began to drag Laurel down the slopping side of the boulder towards him. 
“So . . . you think me being in the pool with all my clothes on is funny?” Maynard said in a teasing voice. “Well . . . let’s see how much you like it.” 
“Please.” Laurel begged. “No . . .” 
But before she could get another word out, Maynard had accomplished what he had set out to do. Laurel landed with a splash into the pool. Maynard had released her leg just as she fell and now he treaded water a ways away from her as she came sputtering and gasping to the surface. 
“You . . . how could you?” Laurel sputtered as she struggled to get her face clear of all her strings of wet hair. “God . . . that was a terrible thing to do to me!”
“Was it?” Maynard went on in a teasing voice. “It seems to me you asked for it . . . laughing at me the way you did.” 
Laurel continued to sputter and fume as she made her way over to him. Maynard could’ve easily evaded her, if that had been what he wanted. She finally reached him and continued to sputter. 
“How could you?” Laurel pushed water in his face again and again. “You got me all wet.” 
“Yes, I did.” Maynard replied calmly as he reached out with both hands and brought her up close against his chest. He encircled his arms around her with her arms under his, so she couldn’t splash at him any more. His powerful legs kept constantly treading water to enable him to keep them both afloat without the use of his arms. 
Laurel found herself facing Maynard, pinned against his chest with his powerful arms, neck deep in water, and unable to move. A slow grin spread across Maynard’s face. Laurel stuck her tongue out at him. 
“So . . . I guess you can’t really avoid dealing with me any longer . . . can you?” Maynard asked with a grin. 
“No.” Laurel stuck her tongue out at him again. “I guess I can’t.” 
“So . . . what are you going to do?” Maynard asked, his face growing serious. 
Laurel looked at Maynard. He was gorgeous. He was also being a beast. So she did what she always did, when she was pissed and couldn’t do anything else. She cried. Tears formed at the edges of her eyes and slowly slid down her cheeks to join the water in the pool. She closed her eyes and laid her head on Maynard’s soggy t-shirted shoulder and cried. 
Now, it was Maynard’s turn to not know what to do. He looked around with a bit of a helpless look. 
“Don’t cry.” Was all he could think of to say, but he didn’t let her go. If he did, she might get away and they’d never get this settled. 
“Why not?” Laurel murmured into his wet shoulder. “I . . . can’t think of anything else . . . to do.” 
“You could talk to me.” Maynard suggested helpfully. 
“I don’t want . . . to talk to you.” Laurel stammered around a sob. 
“Why not?” Maynard kept at it. 
“Because I don’t . . . know . . . what to say.” Laurel mumbled. 
“Why not?” Maynard returned. 
“Because I feel like a fool . . . a complete and utter fool.” She sniffled loudly. “I feel like a fool . . . I don’t know what to say . . . and you think I’m a fool, too.” 
“No, I don’t.” Maynard replied quietly. 
“Oh . . . yes you do.” Laurel replied a bit more clearly this time. “How could you not?” 
“Because I care about you. No matter what you ever did, I wouldn’t think you were a fool.” Maynard spoke seriously. “I’d know you’d have good valid reasons for whatever you did. Even if it was hard for me to understand, if I viewed it from your perspective, I’d see you were no fool.” He laughed. “Besides . . . in this instance . . . I think I’m the fool.” 
Laurel had quit crying. She lifted her head off Maynard’s shoulder and looked him in the eye. 
“How so?” She asked. “How are you a fool?” 
“Well . . .” Maynard grinned at her. “I’m treading water with my legs while I hold a crying woman still against her will. That doesn’t sound too reasonable to me. Does it to you?” 
“No.” Laurel looked at him questioningly. “No . . . that’s a bit of a bizarre kind of thing for you to be doing.” 
“There . . . finally . . . we agree on something.” Maynard paused and looked hopefully into her face. “Please . . . can we just talk . . . quietly and calmly?” He glanced around. “Someone may come by and see me like this . . . being a fool.” 
“OK.” Laurel relented hesitantly. “We can talk . . . but where?” 
“Right here.” Maynard just barely perceptively began to release her arms. “I can move a lot faster than you, so you can’t get away. I can’t let that happen . . . otherwise we’ll never get this settled. And I think it’s important that we do.” 
“You want me to talk to you here in the water?” Laurel couldn’t believe it. 
“Yes.” Maynard released her arms and put them up around his neck. “See . . . you just hang onto my neck.” He looked her in the eyes. “And . . . you just wrap your legs around my waist . . . and I’ll keep us both afloat with my legs.” 
“You want me to . . . what?” Laurel was not sure she had heard right. 
“Put your legs around my waist.” Maynard grinned at her. “Or . . . would you rather dog paddle while you talk to me?” 
“Thank you . . . but no.” Laurel made a face at him. “I’ll take the waist.” 
With careful consideration of everything she was close to, Laurel gingerly wrapped her legs around his waist. When she looked at Maynard, she found him smiling down into her eyes. 
“What are you so happy about?” Laurel questioned him. 
“Well . . . I’ve got you where I want you. In a place where you can’t get away.” His smile turned into a grin. “And . . . I have a beautiful lady up close with her legs wrapped around me. What man wouldn’t be happy?” 
Laurel reached a hand back and whapped water into his face. 
“Hey . . . none of that . . . or I’ll have to take your arms prisoner again.” Maynard grinned at her again. “And . . . I don’t know about you . . . but I like your arms when they’re wrapped around my neck much better.” 
Laurel slowly put her arms back up around Maynard’s neck and watched his face. She could feel his legs churning the water beneath them to keep them afloat. He was expending a lot of energy to keep her here like this. 
“So . . .” Laurel spoke, watching his face closely. “What do you want to talk about?”
“I want to talk about this rumor I just heard . . . about how you find me so attractive . . . that it’s complicating your life a bit.” Maynard said with a grin. 
“Oh . . . that.” Laurel stuck her tongue out at him. 
“You do that again.” Maynard laughed. “And I’m going to suck it right into my mouth.” 
Laurel leaned back quickly and watched him carefully. She had the distinct feeling that she should watch herself. This was definitely not the teddy bear she knew. While Maynard was still the same, he had changed, too. And she wasn’t sure in what ways and how much. 
“OK.” Laurel looked at him carefully. “I won’t stick my tongue out at you anymore.” 
“Ah . . . shucks.” Maynard said, grinning at her. “And I was so hoping you would.” 
Before Laurel realized what she was doing, she started to stick her tongue out at him again. Without thinking, she had just reacted to him. She realized almost instantly what she was doing. She tried to stop, but it was too late. Maynard was fast. Damn fast.
Behind his grin, Maynard had been watching Laurel carefully. And when he saw his chance, he made his move. With lightening speed, his mouth caught hers with her tongue still partially out. And, true to his word, he sucked it into his mouth. 
Laurel gasped, but he didn’t release her. Surprised at herself, Laurel found herself resisting him for a moment. Then she gave up and kissed him in return. The moment she did, he stopped. 
Instantly Maynard released her. Suddenly Laurel found herself alone in the water, dog paddling to keep from sinking. Laurel looked around to see where Maynard had gotten to and found him over by one of the boulders, hanging onto it with one hand and watching her closely. Laurel swam over to him and dog paddled in front of him.
“What’s this?” Laurel asked him. “I’m going to have to dog paddle for myself for the rest of our talk . . . is that it?” She looked intently at Maynard. “A simple ‘please dog paddle’ would have been nicer than just dumping me in the pool . . . don’t you think?” Laurel asked him. 
“Well . . .” Maynard said, still watching her carefully. “When one finds a hot potato in one’s hand . . . one drops it . . . and drops it fast.” 
“A hot potato?” Laurel spoke slowly while watching Maynard. “In my life . . . I’ve been called many things. But being called a ‘hot potato’ . . . is definitely a first for me.”
“Well . . .” Maynard replied hesitantly. “You know what I mean.” 
“I don’t think I do.” Laurel looked questioningly at him. “Why don’t you tell me what you mean?” 
“God . . .” Maynard frowned and wiped his hand across his face. “What have I gotten myself into?” 
“I don’t know.” Laurel said, a small grin lighting her eyes. “But . . . I’m getting tired of dog paddling.” She caught and recognized Maynard’s look as he glanced at her, but she had no mercy. 
“I think I want to be in that much more comfortable position . . . while you explain yourself.” Her eyes twinkled at him. “You know . . . the one with my legs wrapped around your waist and my arms wrapped around your neck.” 
“Are you serious?” Maynard’s mouth almost gaped at her. 
“Yes.” Laurel grinned. “Turn about is fair play. Come on over here.” 
Maynard swam out a ways and Laurel once again carefully wrapped herself around him. 
“There.” Laurel grinned at him. “Isn’t that better? Well . . . it is for me. Now . . . explain this ‘hot potato’ business.” 
“Well . . .” Maynard looked a bit uncomfortable. “You wouldn’t talk to me. So . . . I thought I’d just get you to talk to me and we’d work it out. Then . . . I thought you were probably not truly attracted to me. After all . . . when I wanted you to wrap your legs around me before, you didn’t want to do it. So I thought . . . if I could trick you just a little . . . into getting a chance to kiss you . . . in a way that was kind of all in fun . . . you’d fight me and realize that when it really came down to it . . . you didn’t find me that attractive.” Maynard stopped talking and watched Laurel carefully. “When I started to kiss you . . . you resisted for a moment . . . just as I had thought. But then . . . you kissed me back. So I dropped you.” 
“I see.” Laurel commented quietly. “Why did you drop me?” 
“I dropped you because . . . you had instantly become a bit of a hot potato.” Maynard said rather sheepishly. 
“And why did I instantly become like a hot potato to you?” Laurel pressed on.
“Well . . . two reasons really.” Maynard watched her very carefully. “Because when you stopped resisting and kissed me back . . . I instantly realized that you really were attracted to me.” 
“I see.” Laurel pondered this news for a moment. “And what was the second reason I instantly became a hot potato?” 
“Because . . .” Maynard wasn’t sure he should go on. But he had a feeling, and rightly so, that Laurel wouldn’t let him get away without answering. “The same instant I found out that you were really attracted to me . . . I realized that I wanted to do a lot more than just kiss you.” 
Maynard was watching Laurel carefully. He saw the instant she realized what he was saying. Then he saw her remember where she was, wrapped around his body.
He had no idea how Laurel would react to the bit of news he’d just given her. He had known her a long time and he cared a great deal about her, but he still didn’t know what to expect. What he had just uttered complicated their lives instantly. It was one thing for her to find him attractive, and she’d been having trouble dealing with that. Now he had sprung this last bit on her, out of the blue. The ramifications of what had just transpired were like ripples on a pool when someone throws a pebble in. Unending.
He had known this lady had guts. He had seen evidence of it before. As he watched her now, he could see her digesting his bit of information and taking in their present situation. 
“Well . . .” Laurel looked intently into Maynard’s eyes. “It seems we have a problem then.” She said as she watched his face. “Thank God, I’m in this mess with a friend.” And without another word, she leaned towards him and kissed his cheek. Like he had known, she had guts. 
“Yes.” Maynard smiled at her. “Now that you mention it . . . that is a big plus for us.” 
“Yes . . . but what do we do?” Laurel asked the question that was in his mind, too.
“Maynard.” Laurel watched him carefully. “I know how much you care about me . . . how much we’ve shared in the past. You’re not going to blame yourself for the position we’re in . . . are you?” She looked at him closely. “You know . . . your duty to me as your creator and all that stuff.” She tried to read his face. “We’re agreed that you’re an individual and have total freedom of choice. That we’re equal friends . . . definitely not creator and something she created.” 
“I know what you mean.” Maynard looked seriously at her. “And I think we settled all that a long time ago. We’re equal friends that find ourselves in an unusual situation we have to solve.” 
“OK . . . good.” Laurel smiled at him. “Then . . . it’s on to the problem of what we should do.”
“Yes.” Maynard looked intently at Laurel. “I seem to have taken the large problem of your finding me attractive . . . and compounded that problem by adding my own attraction for you into the equation.” He grinned. “Sorry about that. But . . . you are very beautiful . . . and I do care a great deal about you.” 
“Actually . . .” Laurel said, grinning back at him. “You may have made it easier in a way.”
“Really?” Maynard asked. “How?” 
“Well . . . the main reason I was having difficulty talking to you about my attraction for you . . . was because I felt like a fool.” Laurel frowned. “I felt like I ought not to feel attracted to you.” She smiled at him. “But . . . with you being attracted to me . . . it helps. I know the same reasons that I find you attractive cause you to find me attractive. So . . . I no longer feel like a fool.” She grinned. “Now . . . I can talk to you about it. But . . . you’re right . . . it does make the problem a bit more critical.” 
“Yes.” Maynard spoke up. “And . . . with your legs wrapped around me like this, it might just get real critical.” 
“Oh . . .” Laurel grinned. “I don’t care if it does make the whole overall problem harder to solve . . . I’m glad you find me as irresistible as I find you.” 
“So . . . what do we do?” Maynard asked quietly. 
“Well . . . we have two choices really . . . possibly three.” Laurel leaned towards him, kissing him on the cheek. “We can do nothing. Now . . . this would be a hard choice. Since we spend so much time together and we frolic on the beach in the all together, it would appear that the choice of doing nothing wouldn’t be an all together pleasant one.” She gazed at him. “Unless . . . of course . . . we suddenly found each other unattractive.” 
“I seriously doubt . . . that that will happen.” Maynard replied slowly. “Right now . . . I’m striving . . . to be a gentleman. But . . . I’d very much like to kiss you again . . . and run my hand up here like this . . . and . . .” Maynard sighed, and reluctantly stopped his hand from where it had been swiftly sliding up her leg. “I just wanted you to know . . . in case you think that any of this will go away.” 
“Thank you . . . for that bit of helpful information.” Laurel grinned at him. “And . . . just so you know . . . I want to slide down your body and . . . oh . . . I best not do that.” Laurel said as she found herself sliding down even as she spoke. And, from what she had felt, she had decided she shouldn’t inform him further. 
“No.” Maynard spoke with visible effort. “You . . . best not do that.” He grinned at her. “I’m new at this sort of thing. And . . . I don’t have much experience . . . denying myself the pleasure.” He gazed down into her eyes with a hungry look. “I haven’t had the cause to . . . up until now. So I don’t know yet . . . how good I am at denying myself what I want.” He grinned. “But . . . if this is any indication, I don’t think I’m going to be very good at this denying stuff . . . it’s too hard.” He said and they both had to laugh. 
“So . . .” Maynard strove to continue to solve their problem. “What’s our second choice?” 
“Oh . . . that one is easy . . . very easy to do.” Laurel smiled at him. “We just do whatever we want . . . whenever we want to do it. But . . .” She frowned. “This choice would have the most far reaching ramifications of any choice. I could never do that to Rowan. Not in a million years. So . . . it’s a definite ‘no’ to choice number two for me.” 
“Well, since I happen . . . unfortunately at this moment . . . to care about you and respect you.” Maynard spoke carefully. “I guess I have to agree with you . . . and say ‘no’ to choice number two as well. Besides, just as you said, I wouldn’t do that to Rowan either.” He grinned. “But . . . the doing of it would be fun.”
“Yes.” Laurel looked at him seriously. “The doing of it would be very fun. I just wouldn’t be able to live with myself . . . afterwards.” 
“Yes . . . I know.” Maynard said just as seriously. “I’ve watched you and Rowan together enough to know that . . . as much as you obviously enjoy sex . . . you love Rowan even more. You’d never to anything to hurt him.” Maynard acknowledged. “Does that mean we have no choice . . . but to do nothing?” 
“Yes and no.” Laurel watched his face. “Yes . . . in that we can do nothing now. But no . . . in that the possibility of choice number three exists.” 
“What’s choice number three?” Maynard asked hopefully. 
“It involves me talking at length and at great depth with Rowan.” Laurel spoke carefully. “I need to get his suggestions as to what would be the best thing . . . for you and me to do. You see . . . he knows me very well. And he cares about you a lot. He’s also a realistic man. He realizes how much Amelia’s changes have affected him as well.” A thoughtful look came to Laurel’s face. 
“It could very well be that Rowan and Amelia will eventually face much the same choices as we do. I know they’re attracted to each other. Maybe it’s only a matter of time . . . before they face just this same dilemma.” Laurel paused, searching Maynard’s eyes. “Maybe this is a problem that they’ll need to solve . . . as much as we will.” She grinned. “We’ve just stumbled onto the problem first.” 
“You could be right.” Maynard commented thoughtfully. “I know the attraction is there. He created her . . . much as you created me.” 
“Well . . . we’ll just have to put all this on hold until Rowan and I get a change to figure out what to do. And . . . depending on what your relationship is with Amelia . . . you might want to discuss this with her.” Laurel added quietly. 
“Possibly.” Maynard said thoughtfully. “We’re just starting to build a relationship together. But as I’ve observed in Rowan and your relationship . . . honestly does seem the best way to go.” 
“Yes . . . it’s good to be honest.” Laurel spoke very seriously. “It’s the only way two persons can stay totally connected . . . which is how Rowan and I like to be.” 
“So . . . that’s choice number three, is it?” Maynard made a grimace. “A wait and see approach.” 
“Yes.” Laurel gave a grimace, too. “Sorry . . . but I think that’s the best we can do now.” 
Laurel leaned forward and kissed his cheek. She began to move her mouth to kiss him gently on the lips, but Maynard put up a hand and stopped her. 
“I know you’re just showing me . . . you care.” Maynard said hesitantly. “And . . . don’t get me wrong . . . in any other circumstance . . . I’d like that and appreciate it.” He paused. “But right now . . . I think it would be best . . . if you don’t.” He let a small smile light the edges of his lips, but it was a sad smile. “I’d very much like to convince myself that I could kiss you . . . and then stop it there. But . . . I’m not sure. Not sure enough to risk what would happen . . . if I got carried away.” He leaned back and separated himself reluctantly from her body. “I think I’d just . . . better not.” 
“OK.” Laurel moved away from him. “But . . . I do care a great deal about you.” 
“I know.” Maynard smiled. “Me, too.”
Slowly they made their way to the edge of the boulders. Maynard had to get out first and then reach down and help Laurel out. The sides could provide a person with a way out, but it was difficult. They looked a mess when they climbed out. 
“I wonder what Rowan and Amelia will think when they see us.” Maynard commented as they made their soggy way to the Dance Floor. 
“It doesn’t much matter.” Laurel replied quietly. “They’ll probably think I fell in and you rescued me.” She smiled at him. “And that’s not too far from the truth. Besides . . .” She reached out and squeezed his hand. “I’m going right home and tell Rowan all about it anyway.”
Maynard put his hand out and stopped her. “My thoughts will be with you.” He spoke carefully. “I’d offer to go with you . . . but I know you don’t need me there or want me there. You and Rowan will want to solve this problem together . . . by yourselves.” He smiled slightly. “But later . . . after you decide how to solve the problem, let me know if it would help for me to talk to him.” 
“Thanks, Maynard, I will.” Laurel paused and she almost reached out to hug him like she would’ve normally done up until a few days ago. 
“It’s all right.” Maynard had caught her look. “You can still hug me.” He watched her face. “I think right now . . . I could use a hug, too.” 
So in the middle of the meadow with Rowan and Amelia looking on and wondering what the hell they had done to get all wet, Laurel and Maynard hugged each other long and hard. 

* * *

Rowan and Laurel walked across the meadow towards the dome that held the hot tub. As soon as Laurel had reached Rowan by the Dance Floor where he and Amelia had been talking and drinking sodas, Laurel had asked if he’d like to soak in the hot tub with her. She had explained that she and Maynard had ended up in the pool and a soak in the hot tub would feel great right then. Rowan didn’t ask if Amelia and Maynard wanted to join them. After the way he and Amelia had left Laurel and Maynard, he wanted to find out if Laurel was OK. 
Laurel slogged through the meadow towards the hot tub. Rowan knew her well enough to realize she would talk to him when she was ready. It was not until later as they sat side by side in the hot tub, that Laurel felt ready enough to begin. She felt much better since she’d been able to get out of her wet clothes. Sipping slowly at her wine, she watched the bubbles swirling around them and wondered how to begin. There was no question in her mind that she’d tell Rowan everything that had happened. And right away. She could no more be with Rowan and not have the truth between them then she could have quit breathing. 
“After you and Amelia left to get drinks, Maynard got desperate to find a way to get me to talk, so he pulled me into the pool.” Laurel began at the only place she could, at the beginning. 
“I bet that got you talking.” Rowan smiled at her. “Shouting, more than likely.”
“Yes, it did.” Laurel returned his smile faintly. “Then . . . one thing led to another . . . and Maynard kissed me.” She paused. “Kind of in a teasing way. I resisted for a second or two . . . and then I started to kiss him back. As soon as I did that . . . he dropped me like a hot potato.” 
“I see.” Rowan said seriously. He watched Laurel’s face carefully as she began again from the moment he had left and related every detail, ending with the long hug in the meadow. 
“So . . .” Laurel came to the end of her recital. “That’s the best I could do . . . settle for choice number three.” 
“The ‘wait and see’ choice.” Rowan watched her face, and then he turned to watch the bubbles boiling to the surface all around them. 
“You and Maynard may be right about me and Amelia, too.” Rowan commented thoughtfully. “I know I feel an attraction for her. Like you . . . I can hardly help it. And she seems to like me well enough. If she were to kiss me like Maynard did you . . . I’m not so sure I could’ve settled for coming away with choice number three.” 
“Yes . . . I know what you mean.” Laurel smiled at him. “We seem to be both faced with the same problem. Mine just came to the surface sooner.” She paused, playing with the bubbles. “What do we do? I can’t imagine what it would be like to work as closely with Maynard as I have in the past and feel attracted to him like I do . . . and find a way to deal with not doing anything.” 
“Why should you do nothing?” Rowan asked Laurel quietly. “Why should you say ‘no’ to him? If you want to say ‘yes’ to him, why don’t you?” 
“Well . . . today I said ‘no’ because I don’t ever want to do anything that would hurt you.” Laurel replied quietly. 
“Yes . . . I know.” Rowan leaned down to kiss her forehead. “And I appreciate your concern. I’ve always hoped that if anything like that happened unexpectedly . . . I’d be able to deal with it. But talking about dealing with it and dealing with it are two very different things.” 
“Yes, I know.” Laurel murmured. 
“But I know now how much you feel attracted to each other.” Rowan turned Laurel’s face to his. “Why now that I know . . . don’t you just say you’re going to do it . . . and then . . . just do it?” 
“I’d still think I would hurt you in some way.” Laurel searched her thoughts. “I’d be afraid you might think I cared less about you.” 
“Well . . . if you did make love to Maynard . . . would you care less about me?” Rowan asked her. 
“No. I will always love you.” Laurel answered truthfully. 
“When Maynard was a teddy bear I knew you loved him and cared about him. There’s no reason to expect that those feelings would go away . . . just because he’s a man.” Rowan paused. “I knew that when you loved him as a teddy bear, you still loved me. So by rights . . . I shouldn’t mind it . . . if you love him as a man.” 
“Yes . . . intellectually you can say that.” Laurel pondered. “But . . . if I actually make love to him . . . would you still think it was OK for me to love him?” 
“Wait . . .” Rowan climbed out on the edge of the hot tub to sit across from her. “Let’s approach this from a different perspective.”
“If I were to make love to Amelia . . . would that bother you?” Rowan asked seriously.
“Yes.” Laurel replied hesitantly. 
“Why?” Rowan asked quietly. 
“Because . . . well . . . when I think about it . . . it wouldn’t mean you didn’t love me. It would just mean that you loved her . . . or were in very deep lust.” Laurel grinned at him. 
“Good . . . keep going.” Rowan encouraged her. “Is there something that you wouldn’t like about it?” 
Laurel thought about it for a while . . . a long while. And Rowan waited. 
“I guess that the only thing I’d really not like would be the fact that you were with her . . . and not with me.” Laurel went on carefully. “I like always being around you no matter what we’re doing.” 
“So . . . it’s the time I wouldn’t be with you that would be important.” Rowan thought a moment. “What if I was with you and her at the same time? Then I wouldn’t be spending time away from you.” 
“Now . . . you’re getting kinky.” Laurel laughed. 
“Why?” Rowan asked quietly. 
“Because . . . what would I do? You could only, technically, be with one of us at a time. I don’t believe I could just watch you do it with someone else.” Laurel replied seriously. 
“Well . . . what if you were so busy with Maynard . . . you didn’t have time to notice or mind much . . . that I was otherwise occupied?” Rowan asked with a grin. 
“Oh . . . I see what you mean.” Laurel grinned. “Neither one of us would feel left out . . . if we were busy ourselves.” She paused as her expression changed to a serious one. “It might work . . . if I could be sure that you were sure I loved you. That you were sure of my feelings towards you . . . even if I loved Maynard, too.” Laurel looked at Rowan closely. “You know me very well. You know that if I make love to someone like Maynard, who I already care deeply about, I’m very likely to end up loving him, too.” She watched Rowan carefully. “Since I care about him . . . I could very well begin to love him, too.” 
Rowan levered himself up and into the water again, sinking down until his face was level with Laurel’s and only inches away. 
“I think you already do.” Rowan said quietly. 
“Do you really think so?” Laurel asked hesitantly. 
“Well . . . I’ve known you a great many years.” Rowan went on carefully. “And you never find anyone even remotely attractive unless they have certain qualities inside that are important to you. And you built all those qualities into Maynard.” 
“Yes . . . I did, didn’t I?” Laurel spoke with a bit of wonder in her voice. 
“When Maynard was a teddy bear you could safely show your affection in hugs and kisses.” Rowan continued slowly. “But now you have those same feelings for a man . . . a man whose physical appearance is very attractive to you.” He found Laurel’s hands in the water and pulled her to him. “It’s very easy to fall in love with a best friend.” Rowan kissed her lightly. “Remember, you’ve done it once already.” 
“Yes, I have . . . haven’t I?” Laurel kissed him back. “And you did, too. And look at us.” She grinned at him. “Still very much in love.”
“That’s what happens when the person you love also happens to be your best friend.” Rowan spoke quietly. 
“You seem to be very understanding of my feelings towards Maynard.” Laurel commented. 
“Of course, I am.” Rowan smiled at her. “Just think back over what we’ve just said and take out your and Maynard’s names . . . and insert mine and Amelia’s. It comes out just the same.” 
“It does, doesn’t it?” Laurel commented softly. “So . . . why did this problem affect Maynard and me before it did you and Amelia?” 
“That’s easy to answer.” Rowan answered quietly. “While I know Amelia well and care a great deal about her . . . she has never been a physical presence to me before.” He paused, watching her carefully. “While you and Maynard have always interacted physically on a personal caring level. It’s just now changed to include a sexual element.” Rowan continued. “It’s only a matter of time until I’m faced with the same problem with Amelia. After all . . . I created her with the characteristics and talents I value in a person and now she’s turned out to be a beautiful lady.” A grin lit his eyes. “A red haired freckle faced lady . . . at that.” 
“Yes, I know.” Laurel grinned at him. “I’m surprised you haven’t jumped her bones already . . . for that reason alone.” 
“Believe me . . . I’ve thought about it.” Rowan grinned, and then his face grew serious.
“Listen . . . Laurel.” Rowan went on quietly. “I loved the idea of the Amelia we were eventually able to create. I care about Amelia and respect her knowledge and abilities and the way she runs our ship. I care about her enough to want her to be an independent being. And now . . . she’s chosen to be a beautiful woman. How could I not grow to love her?” He looked intently into Laurel’s eyes. “But that doesn’t mean I love you any less.” He paused. “You do understand what I’m saying, don’t you?” 
“Yes.” Laurel carefully watched Rowan’s face. “I understand you and believe you . . . because I know I love you dearly and I can still see the possibility that I love Maynard, too. I admit the way I love Maynard is different from the way I love you. Our love has grown everyday through our years and years of living together and sharing our lives. But I imagine that just as I love you more the longer I know you . . . it’s possible that I’ll grow to love Maynard more, too.” 
“Strange . . . isn’t it?” Rowan commented quietly. “One of the reasons I care so much about Maynard myself . . . is because of the way he cares about you and because of the traits you gave him.” He paused. “I can almost see a time in the future when . . . if you continue to grow to love him more . . . that I will, too.” 
“Yes . . . it is strange.” Laurel responded. “You’d think that if you were talking about making love to another woman, I’d be filled with jealously. But . . . I like Amelia. She’s nice and she cares about you and me so much.” Laurel smiled and kissed Rowan. “But it follows . . . doesn’t it? If I love you and you choose to love someone else, too. Chances are . . . I’ll love them as well. I can’t help, but. After all if the person you love loves you back, they must have extremely good taste.” 
“You’re right.” Rowan returned her smile. 
“So . . . what do we do with Maynard and my choice number three?” Laurel asked him. 
“We do the only thing we can do.” Rowan reached out and took her hand and pulled her out of the hot tub. “We go and talk to them . . . honestly and truthfully.” 
“I know you’re right.” Laurel climbed slowly out of the warm water. “After all . . . what we decide affects Maynard. And if what we think is true . . . what we decide will eventually affect Amelia as well.” 
“Well . . . I guess we go to the Beach Shack and pay them a visit.” Rowan stated quietly. “But first I want to remind you of something.” 
“What?” Laurel answered as she dried off. 
Rowan came over and took her in his arms, towel and all. 
“That I love you very much . . . and that that will never ever change.” Rowan stated quietly. 
“I know.” Laurel answered softly. “Me, too.” 
“Then . . . with that settled . . . we’d best get this problem solved.” Rowan released her and started to get dressed.
They dressed quickly in shorts and t-shirts and started for the door.
“Rowan.” Laurel spoke hesitantly. “This isn’t going to be as easy as it sounds . . . is it?” She came up to him, where he had stopped inside the door. 
“No. It never is. Not when you’re dealing with people’s emotions.” Rowan looked down into her eyes. “Up until now . . . we’ve had our two sets of feelings to deal with and there’ve been things we had to work out.” He paused. “But we worked them out and now it’s pretty much clear sailing for us. It’s bound to make things more complicated adding two more sets of feelings into the equation. So . . . there’ll be things we’ll have to work out.” Rowan took her hand and they started out the door and down the path that led to the beach. “But . . . we’ll work them out the same way we worked out the others . . . by talking . . . honestly and truthfully.” He smiled into the darkness. “It’s been a pretty good adventure the two of us have had. I wonder what it will be like when we add in the two of them.” 
“Yes, I know.” Laurel answered as they walked along. “They’re very special.” 
“Yes, they are.” Rowan commented quietly. “I wonder if the fact that they’re AIs will make it easier or harder.” 
“I don’t know.” Laurel replied softly. “I guess we’ll just have to wait and see.”


## Chapter 8 — FOURSOME




It was nearly dinner time, when Rowan and Laurel reached the Beach Shack. For the first time that they could remember, they knocked on the door to the Beach Shack. From inside they heard a loud “come in” and they opened the door and walked in. Maynard and Amelia were seated on the bearskin rug in front of a selection of food spread out picnic-style on the floor. 
“I hope we’re not interrupting you at a bad time.” Laurel spoke as the door closed behind them. “We could come back later, if you want.” 
“No . . . come and join us.” Amelia invited warmly. “We can request some more sandwiches. We’re just having a bit of a picnic dinner.” 
“Are you sure?” Rowan asked as he went forward to find a place on the bearskin rug to sit down. 
“Sure.” Maynard got up and made his way towards the Food Dispenser. “I’ll get you some sandwiches . . . any preference on the kind?” 
“Deli.” Rowan requested. “With extra jalapenos . . . thanks.” 
“And . . . what would you like?” Maynard asked with a grin in Laurel’s direction, where she still stood by the door. 
“Ah . . . I’ll take a deli sandwich, too.” She smiled at Maynard. 
“Are you sure you . . . ah . . . wouldn’t like anything else?” Maynard said softly and winked at her. 
“Well . . . since you’re being so generous.” Laurel grinned back at Maynard. “Maybe I’ll take you up on that offer . . . later.” 
“OK.” Maynard’s eyes twinkled at her. “I’ll hold you to that.” 
Laurel made her way towards the picnic spread and sat down across from Rowan, with her back to the crackling fire they had going in the fireplace. She reached out and dipped a chip and munched it slowly. A few moments later, Maynard returned with their sandwiches. He sat down across from Amelia in the place he had vacated earlier and handed Laurel and Rowan their sandwiches. 
“So . . . to what do we owe the pleasure of your company?” Maynard asked as he munched on his grilled cheese sandwich. 
“Laurel and I . . . wanted to talk to you both.” Rowan spoke between bites. “So . . . we just decided to come on over.” 
“Great.” Maynard replied watching Rowan carefully. 
Rowan glanced at Maynard and then glanced towards Amelia, where she sat eating her own deli sandwich. 
“Amelia . . . do you know what happened . . . between Laurel and Maynard this afternoon?” Rowan asked a bit hesitantly. 
“Yes.” Amelia regarded Rowan carefully. “Maynard and I have been talking about what happened . . . trying to come up with a solution that might help.” 
“Did you come up with anything?” Laurel asked quietly. 
“Not yet. So far . . . we’ve just more or less attempted to understand what this problem must mean for you and Rowan.” Amelia glanced at Laurel. “It can’t be easy for you.” 
“No . . . it’s not easy.” Rowan commented and then he paused as a bit of a grin lit his eyes. “It’s rather hard . . . in fact.” 
Laurel caught Rowan’s eye and grinned. Then as she looked away from Rowan, she caught Maynard’s eye and found him grinning at her. Just on the off chance, Laurel glanced quickly at Amelia. Laurel was in time to see a look she knew must have just been on her face, light Amelia’s face as Amelia grinned at Rowan. And Rowan returned Amelia’s grin. 
A smile found its way onto Laurel’s face. Then the smile turned into a grin. Then very swiftly, the grin turned into a laugh. Laurel couldn’t help herself. She just started giggling. The more she tried to stop, the more she laughed. The others looked at her like she had just lost her mind. But she didn’t care. 
“It’s . . . going . . . to work.” Laurel smiled across at Rowan between fits of giggles. “I can . . . tell. It might be hard . . .” And another fit of giggles interrupted her for a moment, and then she went on. “But . . . it’s going to work.” Laurel gestured around at the four of them. “Four minds . . . and every . . . single . . . one of them . . . is in the gutter.” 
Rowan realized what Laurel must have seen and laughed. 
“Well . . . let’s give it a try.” Rowan said when he could talk. 
“We’ve come to talk to you . . . about trying to work out a variation of choice number two.” Rowan spoke loudly so he could be heard over the last of Laurel’s giggles. 
“But choice number two was the ‘do whatever we want anytime’ one.” Maynard spoke in surprise. “Wasn’t it, Laurel?” 
“Yes.” Laurel turned to look at Maynard, a small smile lighting her eyes. “The ‘do whatever we want anytime’ choice.” 
“That’s great.” Maynard commented hopefully. “What variation of that . . . did you come up with?” 
“It’s not easy to explain.” Rowan turned to look closely at Maynard. “And . . . it won’t be easy, either.” Rowan caught Laurel’s smile. “Let me rephrase that. It will be easy to do . . . but the ramifications that result might not be so easy to handle. But Laurel and I are willing to try. We just wanted to see how you both felt about it.” 
“I didn’t know . . . I was included in this.” Amelia glanced at Rowan. 
“Well . . . technically . . . you’re not . . . yet.” Rowan watched Amelia’s face carefully, a small smile lighting his eyes. “But in the future . . . you very well could be. So for the practical reason of not having this same discussion at a later date . . . we include you now.” Rowan searched Amelia’s face. “But rest assured . . . you’re only included to the extent that you wish to be. All this . . . is in no way intended to rush you into anything.” 
“I’m not sure what’s going on.” Amelia answered Rowan slowly. “But it sounds very interesting. Please . . . continue.” 
“This afternoon I think we covered the part about how I’m attracted to Amelia and how Laurel is attracted to Maynard.” Rowan plunged ahead. “Since this afternoon . . . I’ve learned that Maynard is also attracted to Laurel.” Rowan spoke, looking pointedly in Maynard’s direction. Maynard had the grace not to grin too widely at that moment. 
“And . . . I’m attracted to Rowan.” Amelia stated calmly. 
Everyone turned to stare at her. 
“Well . . . it’s only natural.” Amelia went on quietly. “After all . . . all the reasons we know so well from all your explanations, also apply to me as well.” Amelia glanced at Rowan from the corner of her eye and grinned. “Besides . . . Rowan’s statement begged for my part to be added.” She laughed. “And I seriously doubt that the way I feel would have remained a secret for long . . . probably only until the first time Rowan felt attracted to me on the beach.” Amelia grinned at Rowan. 
“I guess . . . Amelia will be included in this sooner than I thought.” Rowan looked across at Laurel to find her grinning at him as well. 
“Well . . . ah . . . where was I?” Rowan looked at them hesitantly. 
“You were just about to explain the variation you’ve come up with for choice number two.” Maynard answered helpfully. “You had just established the fact that we’re all attracted to each other. But, before you go on, would you like a beer? I thought I might get us some drinks. I have a feeling we’re going to end up doing quite a bit of talking.”
“Sure.” Rowan answered gratefully. “Thanks.” 
“Think nothing of it.” Maynard got up and got the beers and returned to pass them out. Everyone had remained silent while Maynard was gone. They didn’t want anyone to have to repeat anything at this stage. Each one in their own way wanted the conversation to proceed without delays. 
“Well . . . when Laurel and I were discussing the problem she and Maynard discovered this afternoon . . . we realized that Amelia and I were probably only a matter of time away from having the same problem ourselves.” Rowan continued. He paused, glancing quickly to see how Amelia was taking all this. When Rowan looked at her, Amelia was just watching him carefully and slowly sipping at her beer. But she did lick her lips just as he glanced her way. Rowan took that to be a good sign. 
“Laurel and I spent a good deal of time talking about the options she and Maynard had come up with.” Rowan paused and sighed. “Any of you . . . feel free to jump in at any time and help me. I don’t have to do all the talking.” Rowan offered hopefully. 
“You’re doing fine.” Laurel assured him. 
“OK.” Rowan gazed in Laurel’s direction. “Laurel and I realized that just because we care about each other doesn’t mean we can’t care about another person. If one of us cares about another person too . . . it doesn’t mean we love the other less.” Rowan paused and looked around. All the others were sitting, carefully listening to his words. “Also we realized that if we care about someone, the chances are the other of us will care about that person, too. Since I love Laurel . . . I’m very likely to care about anyone she cares about, and vice versa.” Rowan paused to sip his beer. “So we decided that choice number two could probably be worked into a good solution . . . as long as lots of honest, truthful talking existed to work out all the ramifications that are bound to follow.” 
“Very well said.” Laurel complemented Rowan, leaning over to kiss him. 
“So . . . let me see . . . if I understand this.” Maynard glanced carefully at Rowan. “You’re saying that Laurel and I can do what we wanted to do this afternoon . . . that you wouldn’t mind.” 
“Well . . . I don’t know if you and Laurel can do what you wanted to do this afternoon.” Rowan watched Maynard’s face closely. “You’d have to ask Laurel.” Rowan glanced at Laurel. “And as for me not minding . . . that’s not exactly what I meant.”
“I would mind . . . but hopefully not too much. If it did bother me a great deal we’d talk about it and try to work it out.” Rowan carefully studied Maynard’s face. “What I meant to say is that I would understand. And . . . since I love Laurel . . . I would trust that whoever she cared about I would care about as well.” 
“I see.” Maynard murmured quietly. 
“Well . . . we’ve been together a long time.” Rowan continued to watch Maynard. “Intellectually we think we can handle it if one or the other of us should choose to show someone else a bit of affection. But how one thinks one will react . . . and how in reality one does . . . might be two different things. That’s why we’ll need to do a lot of talking. But we both feel that we can work it out.” 
“I think . . . I understand.” Maynard replied carefully. “There might be some rough times in the beginning for you both. But . . . with the help of us all . . . we might be able to work it out so we can all choose choice number two.”
“Right.” Rowan went on quietly. “We also realize that you and Amelia may have a relationship that precludes our choosing choice number two. If so . . . we’ll respect your wishes.” 
“Well . . .” Maynard glanced at Amelia. “We were discussing that earlier. It’s not that we don’t care about each other a lot . . . we just don’t have the feelings of exclusiveness that you and Laurel have had all your lives together. So I think it’s safe to say . . . we don’t have a problem with choice number two.” 
“Good.” Rowan commented. “I guess that’s settled for now.” 
“What do we do now?” Maynard asked, watching the faces of those present. 
“Well . . . we were going to take a swim tonight.” Amelia answered Maynard. “Maybe Rowan and Laurel would like to join us.” 
“That sounds like fun.” Laurel glanced at Amelia. 
“Good.” Amelia smiled at Laurel. “What do you say? How about making a mad dash for the beach and letting Rowan and Maynard clean up the picnic mess?” 
“That sounds like a good plan to me.” Laurel answered with a grin of her own. 
And fast, very fast, Amelia and Laurel stood up and stripped out of their clothes. Laurel and Amelia figured if they could distract the men quick enough, they’d be able to make it out of the dome before Rowan and Maynard noticed that they had to clean up the mess. And it worked. Just as they instinctively had known it would. Rowan and Maynard watched spellbound as the women hurriedly disrobed. Then after a bit of short hand that amounted to a whispered word and a few gestures, the women did something that blew the men away. 
With grins on their faces and mischief gleaming in their eyes, the women walked slowly over to the men. And without a word, Amelia kissed Rowan at the same moment Laurel kissed Maynard. Then Laurel and Amelia turned and raced out the door. 
“I do believe . . . we’ve been had.” Rowan got up and began to clean up the mess. 
“I think you’re right.” Maynard joined him, taking an armful of picnic leftovers to the Recycle Slot. 
“Is it always like this?” Maynard asked with a slight grin. 
“It’s always like this . . . and every other way you can imagine.” Rowan answered as he placed the last bit of trash in the Recycle Slot. “If there’s one thing I can guarantee you . . . it would be that it will never be boring. It never is where women are concerned.” 

* * *

Moonlight reflected off the waves as they gently rolled into the shore. Amelia or Laurel must have requested that the waves be turned down. Maynard and Rowan had left their clothes at the Beach Shack. They walked slowly down the beach towards where they could make out the forms of the women floating out in the ocean in the moonlight. 
“Do you really think this will work?” Maynard turned to look at Rowan.
“Laurel and I think it has a good chance.” Rowan answered looking intently at Maynard. “But . . . it won’t be easy.” He paused. “Sure the first part will be. Whenever the first part happens, it will be easy. But then the morning after comes.” Rowan watched Maynard’s face. “I don’t know how much you know about morning afters . . . but they’re very important . . . very important both to you and to the women you care about.” 
They had reached the beach opposite where the women floated. 
“Could we talk a moment?” Maynard asked quietly. 
“Sure.” Rowan sat down in the sand so that the waves lapped at his outstretched toes. 
“Well . . .” Maynard began hesitantly. “I know a lot about sex, techniques and methods and different ways to do it . . . that kind of thing. I know all about that. But I haven’t experienced much . . . just Mabel and Amelia.” He paused and watched the waves for a moment. “I seem to know a lot about how . . . but not much about when and in what way.” Maynard looked at Rowan. “Do you know what I mean?” 
“Yes, I think I do.” Rowan smiled at him. 
“Do you have any advice . . . that might help me?” Maynard asked hesitantly. 
“If you’re talking about women in general . . .” Rowan answered carefully. “The only advice I could give you would be . . . to just enjoy them. And . . . above all . . . talk to them.” He looked at Maynard. “Anything else you want to talk about?” 
“Why are you and Laurel willing to try to work this out?” Maynard asked quietly. “You have such a special relationship. Aren’t you afraid this might affect it?” 
“Oh . . . I pretty much think we’re sure this will affect our relationship.” Rowan answered quietly. “But we feel that working things out . . . while it may be difficult at times . . . will only make our relationship better in the end. When you work through something with another person, chances are you’ll both be much closer because of it.” Rowan’s face grew serious as he watched Maynard’s face carefully. “Besides . . . how much choice did we really have? When you think about it . . . there was almost no way to avoid it.” 
“I’m beginning to think you might be right.” Maynard stood up. “Interesting . . . isn’t it? How one change can affect so many things you didn’t realize it would affect?”
“Yes.” Rowan gazed out into the ocean at the two forms floating in the moonlight. “That’s what makes tonight so special . . . and so very unpredictable.”

* * *

Maynard and Rowan walked slowly out into the surf. As soon as the water was deep enough, they dove under the surface and swam out to where the women were floating in the moonlight. They approached silently. Rowan went over to where Laurel was floating and kissed first one of her breasts and then the other. Laurel came up sputtering. Then seeing who it was, she moved into Rowan’s arms and kissed him. 
“I love you.” Rowan whispered into her ear. “Don’t ever forget that.” 
“I know.” Laurel answered as she kissed his cheek. “I love you, too.”
“Well . . .” Rowan said, releasing Laurel and speaking a little louder. “Amelia . . . how would you like to see those AI fishes you helped design . . . the ones that glow in the dark?” Rowan asked glancing once more at Laurel. 
“That sounds like fun.” Amelia replied as she swam over to meet Rowan. And without another word, Rowan and Amelia swam further out towards the horizon where the coral reefs were. 
Laurel looked after them for a bit, and then looked around wondering where Maynard was. She spotted him a little ways off, standing motionless as the chest high swells flowed around him. Laurel watched Maynard while he watched her for a long moment. Then Laurel went back to floating on her back. 
Silently Laurel gazed at the stars. After a while she closed her eyes and floated for what seemed like a long time. Then she decided she’d swim a bit and maybe after that she’d see what Maynard was doing. Without opening her eyes, Laurel just rolled over while stretching out to begin her first swimming stroke and bumped into something solid.
